A section that differs from its tag passes only when its digest is the recorded one, and is named `amended`; an amended section edited further, a record whose section is identical to its tag, a line out of shape, two lines for one tag and a tag the check does not read all fail, and a section that differs is printed with its digest. The tags and their published release notes keep the original text. The tests `renaming_underscored_names_takes_linear_cpu_time` and `renaming_underscored_names_in_one_declaration_takes_linear_cpu_time` (`crates/una-cli/tests/recovery.rs`, before `..._scales_linearly`) read the processor time of a child of the test binary past its start, not the elapsed time, which read 2.9 and 2.6 times for a doubling inside the parallel test run of a release gate; the sizes and the bound of 2.5 are unchanged.
- **The base code of `una-sys` has a restated cap, and a test now enforces it** (#2115): the base code (the `unsafe`-bearing code outside the feature `solver`) had grown to 1,651 lines, past the 1,594 lines of ADR 0058, with no amendment and no check. A dated amendment of ADR 0058 (2026-11-19) records the breach and the pull requests behind it and restates the cap at 1,733 lines: the 1,651 on `main` plus at most 5 percent. The new test `crates/una-sys/tests/base_size.rs` fails when the count passes the cap, and refuses a `#[path]` or `include!` in the counted files, a build script, and a `solver` module compiled without its feature. No code of the crate changes.
- **The release gates time the cryptography** (#2119, ADR 0133 rules 9 and 10): the gate `timing-channels` of `scripts/release-gates.sh`, and the step of the same name of `scripts/local-ci.sh`, now run, after the timing oracles and in the same release build, the statistical timing tests of `una-crypto` (`cargo test --release --locked -p una-crypto --test timing -- --ignored --test-threads=1 --nocapture`) and its scaling tests (`cargo test --release --locked -p una-crypto --test perf -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture`), which the ordinary test run ignores or, in a debug build, passes without measuring. In the gate the three commands always run and one that fails fails the gate, so the nightly release run holds each statistic in the log of `timing-channels`. Before, no gate ran either file. The timing test of `ct::eq` samples its two classes on one buffer, 64 calls per sample after a warm-up, with the same claim and the same limit: with two buffers their placement in memory differed with the class, and the statistic left the limit, with either sign, on a loaded machine. No behavior of a program changes.
- **The reactor's scaling test counts its steps** (#2124, `crates/una-rt/src/reactor.rs`). The test that registering, waking and removing a wait are O(1) failed the gate `sanitize` on its wall-clock bound under AddressSanitizer. It now counts every access to the table's vectors, in the unit tests only (outside them the vectors are `Vec` itself), and bounds each `insert`, `take` and `remove` by a constant at 1,000 to 100,000 waits; the timing stays as a second check, the shortest of three measurements, and does not run under Miri or AddressSanitizer. The time bound is unchanged.
- **Release notes for readers, `docs/release-notes/<version>.md`** (#2134, for 0.4.0 and 0.4.1: a summary, highlights with examples, the changes that may need an edit, the security fixes and the platforms; the guide tests check every example and compare every output shown with what `una` prints, and fail when the version of `Cargo.toml` has no notes; `scripts/site-bundle.sh` ships them with the kind `notes`, under the neutrality check, and `scripts/doc-links.py` checks their links; `docs/release-gates.md` §4: the release pull request adds the notes of its version)
- **The `sanitize` gate counts the Miri reduction of the #1943 test** (#1943, `scripts/sanitize.sh`). The test of refused steps beside sibling threads makes 200 steps under Miri rather than 50,000, one more mention of `miri` in `una-rt`: the script states 27, where 26 failed the gate on `main`.
- **`Files`: a grant on the current directory reaches its files again, confined as any other** (#2144, `spec/13 §5`, `spec/14 §3` rule 2; a regression of #1459, never released; a security fix). The prefix of `files:.:<modes>` (or of `./` or `a/..`, a path that names the directory the grant is relative to), in a `--grant` flag, in the grants of an executable and in `[run] grants` of a package run as `una run .`, was held as the empty path, which no host resolves, so since #1459 every `Files` operation under such a grant ended with `NotFound` and wrote nothing. In released versions, up to 0.4.0, a `Files` grant on the current directory (`.`, `./`, `a/..` with an empty or `.` base) was not confined against symbolic links: a path under it was opened as written, so a link in that directory was followed out of the grant, for reads and writes; `..` and absolute paths were refused as always. This is fixed: such paths are `Denied`. That prefix is now `.`, which resolves to the directory itself: the operation is given the canonical form of a directory inside it joined with the final component, and a missing directory is still `NotFound` with no path given to the operating system, in `una run` and in native executables. A grant path that goes up two or more levels now means what it says: `..` no longer removes a `..` before it, so `files:../..:<modes>` is the directory two levels up (it was the empty prefix) and `files:../../x:<modes>` is `../../x` (it was `x` in the current directory, in every released version). No other prefix changes.
- **The test of refused steps beside sibling threads runs in a process of its own** (#1943, `una-rt` tests, `scripts/sanitize.sh`). Under `cargo test`, which runs the unit tests of `una-rt` as threads of one process, another test that ran a program lifted the allocator's limit at the end of its run, and for the instant between the allocator's two writes the bytes held read close to the largest count, so about four of the 200,000 fitting steps of this test were refused and the release gates `build` and `mac` failed. The test now starts its scenario in a process of its own, as the other tests of #1943 do; no code of the runtime changes. The `sanitize` gate states 26 mentions of `miri` again, since the scenario no longer runs smaller under Miri, and its list of the tests Miri ignores names the eight tests of #1943 that run in, or start, a process of their own.
- **The cryptography's timing tests time the routine alone** (#2153): the AES-GCM seal test of `crates/una-crypto/tests/timing.rs` prepared its input inside the timed region, differently for its two classes, and the release gate `linux-arm64` failed on it (`aes-128-gcm seal t = -33.87`) with no dependence on the key or the plaintext in the implementation. That test, the two tag-rejection tests and the X25519 test now sample as the `ct::eq` test does: one state for both classes, written before the clock is read by the same work for both, and the first samples discarded. The claims and the limit are unchanged. No behavior of a program changes.
- **Two tests the release gates could not run reliably** (#2144, `crates/una-rt/src/host.rs`, `crates/una-link/tests/scaling.rs`; tests only). The test of a `Files` grant on the current directory assumed the directory Cargo starts a test binary in, and failed in the gate `sanitize`, which starts the binary in the repository's directory; its body now runs in a process of its own, started in a directory the test makes. The scaling test of the linker measured links of 2 to 19 ms against a fixed cost of about 1 ms that varies by half between measurements, so a ratio was mostly noise; it now links 8,000 to 64,000 functions and 16 to 128 relocations per function (19 to 170 ms), with the bound of 2.3 unchanged.

## v0.4.0

Release R0.4, the first release of edition `0.4`, which it ships as a preview under the terms of ADR 0121 (ADR 0139, amendment of 2026-11-10, accepted): a later release may ask for edits to a package of edition `0.4`, always with a diagnostic and a changelog entry. Edition `0.4`, declared in `ita.una`, adds long-running programs with `for ever`, `stop` and a host-driven `step` (ADR 0116), tasks, `within` and `first of` (ADR 0117), HTTP servers with `HttpServer` and a `serve:` grant by address and limits, served by `una run` and `una build` (ADR 0123; an `https` grant fails closed, and `--serve-script` runs only in the toolchain's test builds), and the core of the package manager Itá, `ita.una`, `ita.lock` and `una migrate` (ADR 0122; the registry is designed but not served: packages are read only from a mirror directory, and `publish` and `yank` send nothing). Edition `0.5` is served as an early preview with collections and the numeric foundation, moved there from `0.4` (ADR 0139), and without module constants or `const` and `var` yet. Edition `0.3` stays a preview and changes in a way that may need edits: an `example` declaration is now U0103, and `una fix` rewrites it to `test` (#1930). `una` is one file: the Z3 solver and the native runtime are built into it (ADR 0124), and on macOS arm64 `una build` links with its own linker. The release targets are macOS on arm64 and Linux on arm64 with glibc 2.39 or later (`scripts/release-targets.txt`); on Linux `una build` links through the system `cc`, musl systems are refused, and Linux on x86-64 and Windows are not targets. The installer installs 0.4.0 by default and refuses any release before 0.4.0 (#1819). Security: writing an `Int` of more than 4300 digits as text is refused in O(1) instead of taking quadratic time (#923, advisory UNA-SA-020); the fixes of v0.3.17 (#1071, advisory UNA-SA-017; #1130; #1160, advisory UNA-SA-018) are on this line too; and the bullets marked Security below list the channels this release closes: most are in edition `0.4` constructs that no earlier release shipped, and some also affected earlier releases; the published advisories are in `docs/security/advisories.md`. The bullets of #974 to #990 describe edition `0.5`: those constructs first landed in edition `0.4` and moved to edition `0.5` by #1621 before any release served them. Validation: `docs/releases/0.4.0-validation.md`.

### Editions and the language

- **Profiles and the declared memory budget** (#31, ADR 0109 rules 1, 4, 6 and 7). A package declares its profile, `app`, the default, or `systems`, and its memory budget, from 1 to 4096 MiB: `[package] profile` and `[run] max_memory` in `una.toml` of edition `0.3`, the head line `profile` and the `run` line `max_memory` in `ita.una` of edition `0.4`, where `profile systems` is now accepted. A `systems` package with an entry declares its budget (U7002, U7008). The declared budget is the budget of `una run`, `una test` and the executable `una build` makes; `--max-memory` may lower it, and a value above it is a usage error (exit code 2). The build report and the Core export name the profile, and U2039 and U3032 are registered for the `simd required` clause of ADR 0120. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged.
- **`overflow` obligations on `Int` in `systems` packages** (#31, ADR 0109 rule 3). In a package whose profile is `systems`, each `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, `mod` and unary `-` on `Int` (and on `Int of U`) outside clauses, `convert()` on an `Int` magnitude, calls of `sum` and `abs`, and `to_int()` on `I64` and `U64` carry an `overflow` obligation that the exact result lies in -2^62 to 2^62 - 1, and an `Int` literal outside that range is refuted at once (U5001); the repairs are a guard, a refinement, a fixed-width type, or a wrapping or saturating method of one. The Core program records the profile, and the Core export's header reads it from there. `app` packages are unchanged.
- **The test declaration is written `test` in edition `0.3`** (#740, ADR 0105). An edition `0.3` package writes `test "name"` where editions `0.1` and `0.2` write `example "name"`, with the same lines and rules; `test` is a keyword there, and a local named `test` is renamed by `una fix`. An `example` declaration in an edition `0.3` package is U0103, "`example` is written `test` in edition 0.3", with the exact rewrite of the keyword that `una fix` applies; its body is still read as a test's. `una test` prints `test` and `tests` for such a package, and its `--json` is the schema `una.test/2`: `una.test/1` with the key `examples` renamed `tests`. Diagnostics that name the declaration say "test" in edition `0.3`. Editions `0.1` and `0.2`, `una.test/1` and the Core export do not change.
- **Edition `0.4` opens as a preview** (#888, ADR 0116 rule 0, `spec/26 §1`). `una.toml` accepts `edition = "0.4"`, which has every construct, keyword and rule of edition `0.3`. It is the edition of long-running programs: `diverges` and `within` are reserved in it (U0103), and the constructs of ADR 0116 join it as they land. Earlier editions do not change, and `una --version` lists `0.4`.
- **Canonical soft wrapping in edition 0.4** (#1113, ADR 0125 and its amendment of 2026-10-25, `spec/15 §2`). In edition 0.4, `una fmt` and `una check` wrap a logical line wider than 100 Unicode scalar values after a comma between arguments or elements, at the outermost separator that fits, in time linear in the line's length. This is the canonical form of edition 0.4 only and is not backported: editions 0.1 to 0.3 keep their canonical form, so their code gets no new U7001, and their obligation ids, goals and hypotheses are unchanged. In edition 0.4, obligation goals and hypotheses and `una contract` show source text without the wrapped layout, so wrapping a line changes no obligation id.
- **`example` is an ordinary name in edition 0.4** (#1584, ADR 0105 amendment of 2026-11-05, `spec/01 §5`). In edition 0.4 `example` is neither a keyword nor reserved: it names a function, a parameter, a local, a field or a module path segment, and a line `example "..."` at column 1 is an ordinary syntax error (U0101) with no U0103 and no rewrite. The test declaration is written `test`, as in edition 0.3. `una fix` rewrites `example` to `test` in edition 0.3 packages only, the migration from editions 0.1 and 0.2, which keep `example` as their declaration keyword unchanged. Edition `0.4` preview: an edition 0.4 package that still writes an `example` declaration now gets U0101 at its text literal in place of U0103; migrate it on edition 0.3 with `una fix`, or write `test`.
- **Edition 0.5: collections and the numeric foundation move there from edition 0.4** (#1621, ADR 0139 rules 6 to 8, ADR 0119 and ADR 0120 amendments of 2026-11-06). Edition `0.5` is a new preview that `ita.una` may name: it has every construct of edition `0.4`, plus `std.collections`, `Map.update`, `Decimal[s]`, `F32`, the finite kinds, `Complex`, the dense types and constructors, `simd` as a name and the `with:` and `and:` labels. In edition `0.4` these are what edition `0.3` makes of them (U1001, U0101, U0103), since no release served them there (ADR 0139 rule 7), with the advice to write `edition 0.5`. A package of edition `0.4` checks the same in edition `0.5` but for those constructs, which a new edition test pins.
- **`una --version` names the preview editions, and the report names the edition** (#1621, ADR 0121 rules 7 and 8). `una --version` prints `preview: 0.3, 0.4, 0.5` after `editions:`, and `una.version/1` gains `"preview"` after `"editions"`, `[]` when there is none. `una.report/2` gains `edition`, after `package`, with the package's edition and whether it is a preview, and the human report prints `edition: 0.5 (preview)`. Both keys are additive. A test checks that the previews of `una --version` are the editions that `spec/13 §7` marks as previews.
- **Edition 0.4 advises edition 0.5 for every moved construct** (#1743, ADR 0139 rule 7). `simd` as a name (U0103, after its rename), the scale of `Decimal[s]` and the `with:` and `and:` labels (U0101), and `m.update(...)` on a `Map` (U1001) now carry, in edition 0.4, the advice to write `edition 0.5` in ita.una, as the moved names already did; the errors themselves do not change.
- **`una migrate` reads an edition 0.5 package with the keywords of 0.5** (#1744). A `una.toml` that names edition 0.5 migrates to `edition 0.5`, and its modules are now put in the canonical form read with the keywords of that edition, so a module that uses `simd` as a name or the `with:` and `and:` labels is rewritten, not left as it was.
- **Edition `0.3` preview, a change that may need edits: an `example` declaration is now U0103** (#1930, #740, ADR 0105 rule 3; kind: stricter). An edition `0.3` package that v0.3.16 accepted with `example "adds two numbers"` is now rejected with U0103, "`example` is written `test` in edition 0.3", at each such declaration, so `una check` and `una test` fail on it until it changes. Migration: run `una fix` in the package, which rewrites the keyword, `example "adds two numbers"` to `test "adds two numbers"`, with the same body, and renames a local named `test` to `test_value`, since `test` is a keyword in edition `0.3`; then rerun `una check` and `una test`. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` keep `example` and do not change. A package of edition `0.1` or `0.2` that moves to edition `0.4` with `una migrate` keeps its `example` declarations, which are U0101 in edition `0.4` with no rewrite: set `edition = "0.3"` in its `una.toml` and run `una fix` before `una migrate`, or write `test` by hand.

### Security and soundness

- **An `Int` of more than 4300 digits is never written as text** (#923, security, `spec/11 §7`, in every edition). Interpolation, `to_text`, `to_json`, traces, outputs, failures and test reports write an `Int` in decimal, which takes quadratic time in its digits, while a few multiplications build one of millions of digits. Both engines now refuse such a write before any conversion, in O(1) from the integer's bit length, and abort the run with U6003, `an integer of more than 4300 digits is written as text here, past the limit of a run`, at the interpolation, the call or the operation; the final value or failure of `main` aborts with no position, and a test's report names the value instead. The bound is that of parsing (#914), so every integer a text can write can be written back; #1223 is to lift it with a subquadratic conversion on Una's own big integer (#1093, in this release).
- **Security: a vendored or fetched registry release no longer loads on a provenance whose point is counted by a revoked witness, or by fewer witnesses than a later root version requires** (#1001, ADR 0122 amendment 7c, `spec/30 §9` rule 7, §11 rules 8 and 9). Loading counted the cosignatures of a provenance's checkpoint by the root version the checkpoint names alone, so a witness that a later stored root version lists in `witnesses-revoked` still counted, and so did a threshold lower than the last stored version's. It now counts them as the registry client does: never a revoked witness, and with the larger of the two thresholds. A provenance written by `una ita fetch` was already counted so when the release was fetched, so only a provenance changed after that is refused (U7010).
- **Security: a declaration that does not elaborate to Core IR rejects the package (U8001)** (#1050). A declaration whose elaboration to Core IR stopped without a diagnostic, such as when a resolution the records do not hold is looked up, was left out of Core IR with none of its obligations, so its `requires`, `ensures`, refinements and arithmetic checks were never verified, yet the package was accepted. Elaboration now ends with a cross-check: every type, function, predicate, policy, capability, handler and example of the package that type-checked must be in Core IR, and every contract of the standard library, host capability and prelude kind that Core IR carries must have elaborated; one that is missing is the internal error U8001 and the package is rejected, unless another error already rejects it. A refinement clause that does not elaborate now leaves its declaration out too, so the cross-check finds it, instead of standing in Core IR as a placeholder `false`. No declaration of the prelude, the standard modules or the conformance corpus is dropped today.
- **Function values release what they hold without recursion** (#1071, advisory UNA-SA-017). In the interpreter, a function value releases the values it closes over and the handlers of its capabilities by the same worklist as records, so a value that a loop nests deeply through function values ends normally. Before, releasing such a value recursed on the native stack, and a deep enough one aborted `una run` with a stack overflow, in every release up to v0.3.16. Native executables are not affected.
- **Security: the distrusted emSign root is out of the TLS trust store** (#1130, RUSTSEC-2026-0381, RUSTSEC-2026-0382). `una-http` updates `webpki-roots` from 1.0.9 to 1.0.11, which removes `emSign ECC Root CA - G3`, and `rustls-webpki` from 0.103.15 to 0.103.16, which rejects certificate paths through the emSign intermediates whose private keys are public. A TLS connection made by `una-http` no longer trusts a server certificate issued through them.
- **Soundness: a turn's end no longer depends on earlier code** (#1146, ADR 0117 rule 2, `spec/27 §8` rule 4). Under the real clock, the slicer of native code started only at the run's first started task and then ticked on its own 10 ms phase, so before any `start` tasks that shared a worker never yielded, and after one a turn ended 10 to 20 ms in, by how long earlier code ran. A `secret` condition that decided whether a task was started could so decide the winner of a later `first of` whose label is `public`. A turn was also timed from its start on the worker, so the root, whose turn began at `main`, carried every computation it ran alone before its first `start`, and a `secret` amount of such work could decide whether a later public deadline passed. The slicer now starts with the run's scheduler, whatever the program starts, and each turn ends 10 ms after the later of its start and the moment a second task became ready on its worker: the worker sets the end when it takes a new turn, a task queued behind a lone one moves it, both under the queue's lock, the slicer wakes at the earliest pending end, and the check point's slow path ends the turn only once the clock has reached it, one atomic load and one clock read, with the fast path unchanged.
- **Soundness: the label of a limited request covers its callers' work** (#1147, ADR 0116 P8, slice T1 of its amendment, ADR 0117 IF4, `spec/27 §9` rule 5). `L_lim` covered only the body of the function that holds each request. With `at most 1 per minute`, a second request waits at the bucket for a minute less the time spent since the first, and `--http-timeout` turns that wait into `TimedOut`, so a caller that worked on a `secret` between two calls of the function that makes the request decided, unlabeled, whether the second one was sent. Each request's label now joins `τ⁰` of the body of every ancestor of its function in the call graph of ADR 0116 P1, its own function included, with the preview's `τ_conc`: the graph has the operations of handlers, reached through the operations of their capabilities, and one node for every lambda, reached by every call of a function value, so a handler's operation that works on a `secret` between two requests is covered too. It is computed in linear passes over strongly connected components, and U4001 names the nearest ancestor that raised it, with that body's label. The limits of S5 have not shipped in a release; a program this now refuses was leaking. A two-run differential test, changing only the secret, covers it.
- **Soundness: a canceled task's request no longer changes later timing unlabeled** (#1148, ADR 0116 amendment P8 and P9, `spec/26 §7` rule 5, `spec/27 §2` rule 4, §5, §6, §7 and §9, `spec/24 §3` rule 4). A request whose task was canceled before its own time-out kept its helper, and the cores its exchange spent, until the exchange returned, beside later code that no label covered: whether a started task's request had left when its block failed, which `secret` work before it could decide, changed the outcome of a later `public` deadline. The checker now computes the residue label `L_res`, the join over every `tasks` statement whose parts can reach `Http` of its timing label and its `τ_conc`, and over every `first of` whose branches can reach `Http` of its `t`, through the call graph of ADR 0116 P1 (handlers through their capabilities, lambdas through calls of function values), in one linear pass, and joins it into the timing label of every `within`, `first of`, `for ever within`, tick and `L_lim`; its messages name the construct whose requests can be left running. A program whose started tasks make requests after work on `secret` data now gets U4001 or U4003 at its later timing sites, and one whose started tasks or races make requests has every such site at least `internal`. The runtime bounds the residue too: a task's cancellation now reaches the client, which starts no thread per request: it looks names up on a lookup pool of `max(2, --io-threads / 4)` threads and connects on a connect pool of `--io-threads` threads, both started before `main` in runs granted `Http`, and waits for each only until its deadline or its abandon, runs its exchange on its helper of the I/O pool, keeps its sockets non-blocking there, and stops at its next I/O step, through a shutdown of its socket and with no polling, checking the flag before it finishes parsing or decodes a body; the covering deadline crosses to the helper as an instant, so a request queued behind busy helpers ends at it; a started task that a deadline around its `tasks` statement cancels waits for its helper instead of leaving it, unless its request is still queued, when it is woken at once; requests written directly in lambdas now reach `L_res`; a dependency checked with the package counts precisely, while a reachable standard function that holds a `tasks` statement or a `first of`, or a dependency function whose code is not checked, makes `L_res` `⊤` until #1166; and the TLS client settings are built before `main` in every run whose grants include `Http`, never at a first request. These constructs are part of the edition `0.4` preview; a program this now refuses was leaking.
- **Soundness: deadlines armed and fired in nanoseconds, and the timer thread started before `main`** (#1151, ADR 0116 amendment P8 and P13, `spec/27 §1` rule 4 and §5, `spec/26 §2` and §7). In native code a `within`, an iteration of `for ever within` and a tick of the driver are armed on the monotonic clock in nanoseconds, not whole milliseconds, so each lasts exactly `d` from its arming instead of between `d - 1` and `d` milliseconds by the phase that earlier code, possibly on `secret` data, left; the timing wheel counts nanoseconds, its finest slot one nanosecond wide, and the timer thread sleeps until the next expiration, always a live timer's, and fires each timer at its own nanosecond, never at the end of a millisecond (arming, canceling and firing O(1), at most 10 cascades per timer; removal eager, so canceled timers leave no state and cause no later wake); the driver's tick wait and the `Http` wait of a `for ever within` deadline end at their exact instant instead of the next millisecond, polling the whole milliseconds and sleeping the rest, under 1 ms and uninterruptible. A program with a `within`, a `for ever` or a task construct starts the timer thread with its workers before `main`, so whether an earlier `within` ran no longer changes the cost of the next one; one that cannot start stops the run there with U6003, and a program without these constructs starts no timer thread. Virtual time and `Clock.now` stay in milliseconds.
- **Security: writes under a package no longer follow a planted link** (#1160, advisory UNA-SA-018). `una fmt`, `una fix`, `una migrate`, `una ita lock` and `una fmt ita.una` used to write a temporary file at a fixed name next to the file they replace (`.<name>.fmt-tmp`, `.<name>.tmp`), following a symbolic link at that name. A repository could commit such a link and have a formatting run overwrite a file outside it. The temporary file is now created exclusively, under a name unique to the write, then synced and renamed: for a replacement with mode 0600 until it takes the destination's permissions, and for a new file under the umask, as before. A source that is a symbolic link is still written through onto the file it resolves to, but only when that file and its directory are inside the package: a link that leads outside it is refused, naming where it leads. `una build` creates `target/una` one level at a time, refusing a `target` or `target/una` that leads outside the package, and refuses any link at the executable's name or at `<name>.o`. The linker now writes the executable, beside its object file, in a fresh private directory that `una build` creates inside `target/una` (or inside the directory `--out` names: a plain relative `--out` is rooted at the current directory and created one level at a time in the same way, an absolute one or one with `..` is created as given) and always removes; the executable is then checked to be a regular file there, synced and renamed into place, so a link planted at the final name while the linker runs is never written through. Before, it wrote the object with a write that follows a link, and the linker wrote the final path directly. A `target` deliberately linked to another volume now needs `--out` with an absolute path there, or one with `..`. The proof cache stores its entries the same way, without the fsync since a torn entry fails its tag and is solved again, stores nothing when `.una`, `.una/cache` or `.una/cache/proofs` is a link that leads outside the package, and replaces a link at an entry's name instead of following it, even one that leads inside the package.
- **The proof cache key is used only when it is a private regular file of the user** (#1164): the key file is used when it is a regular file, not a symbolic link, with no other hard link, holding 16 to 4096 bytes, owned by the user, and its mode grants nothing to group or others. Any other existing key file now turns the proof cache off (the report's `tcb.proof_cache_key` is `null`) instead of being used. Each of these refusals (a link, not a regular file, another user's file, a mode that grants group or others anything, another hard link, a size outside 16 to 4096 bytes) prints one note on stderr naming the file and the reason, with a hint where one applies (`chmod 600` it for a mode that grants group or others anything); under `--json` stderr stays empty and the report's `null` is the only signal. A missing key file is made, with no note. The proof cache also reads an entry only from a regular file of at most 64 KiB, and never writes through a link at an entry's name.
- **Security, timing: the warm-up's tasks copy an object at the fork and at the join** (#1254, second part, which stays open until its timing oracle passes on a quiet machine; `spec/27 §1` rule 4, ADR 0116 amendment P8 and P12; edition `0.4`, unreleased). The warm-up of #1255 ran one real task per worker before `main`, but its closure and its value were integer words, so it copied nothing: the copies' pending segment (16 KiB, the largest allocation of a task) and the slot tables of the heaps on both sides were first made by the program's own tasks, as often as earlier code, possibly `secret` code, had started them. The warm-up's closure is now a closure object, exported and imported at the fork, and its value an empty list, exported and imported at the join, as the copies of a compiled block whose tasks return a list, a record or a text are. Measured with per-block counters on `main` 4af0da01, before the fix, at `--workers 1024` with the oracle's program (`a_public_block_s_timing_never_depends_on_how_many_tasks_secret_code_started`): the public block made 36,936 allocations and 19,524,286 bytes under key 0 against 36,935 and 19,524,222 under key 1, with 1,026 to 1,027 against 1,024 to 1,025 minor page faults and no stack mapping or thread start under either; the warm-up block made 24,647 allocations and 2.1 MB with none from 16 to 32 KiB, where each block of the program made 1,024, one per task, the one structural difference found. With the fork half of the fix, the warm-up block made those 1,024 under both keys; the join half was checked by a unit test, not by the counters. Cost: O(N) tasks for N workers once per run, as before, each copying one node in and one out; transient memory bounded by one batch of 64 task heaps, about 1.3 MiB, plus N empty lists in the root's heap, all freed before `main`. The oracle at p >= 0.05 over alternating runs is owed to a quiet machine; the card stays open until it passes.
- **Security: a public block's timing no longer depends on how many tasks `secret` code started** (#1254, native executables, edition `0.4` tasks, unreleased). Before `main`, a run with threads now runs one real task on every worker through the path every block takes, under no budget and leaving no event or object, so what a task's first run on a worker makes and keeps, in the runtime's thread-locals and in the allocator's per-thread caches, exists on every worker whatever earlier code starts. The timing oracle `a_public_block_s_timing_never_depends_on_how_many_tasks_secret_code_started` found the channel on `main` on 2026-10-27; ADR 0116 amendment P8's no-op warm-up had left it.
- **Security: the outer-sandbox check of the solver asks about the loopback network, local sockets and every write at fixed places** (#1363, #1364, #1365, S-31, S-32, ADR 0124 rule 7 and its amendment of 2026-11-01, `spec/13 §6`): on macOS, a solver child under an outer sandbox now takes it as its confinement only if it denies `network-outbound`, `network-inbound` and `network-bind` with no filter, toward `127.0.0.1` and `::1` over TCP and UDP and toward local sockets, and ten write operations (data, create, unlink, extended attributes, mode, flags, times, owner, ACLs and Finder info) at `/`, `/Users`, `/private/var`, `/private/tmp`, `/Volumes`, the account's home directory and the user's temporary directory, none taken from the working directory; before, a profile that allowed connections to `localhost`, writes under the home directory, or ACL, Finder-info, attribute and mode changes was accepted. An answer the library gives for an operation it does not know is U5003, never a denial. Once the outer sandbox is accepted, the child's open-file limit is zero, so no socket or file can be opened. `tcb.solver_outer_denied` names every probe, each denial being the outer sandbox's (profile or system). Signals to other processes and writes that need no descriptor outside those places are accepted residuals the outer profile must deny; `scripts/toolchain-free.sh` and the `hunt` gate deny them. The outer-sandbox tests fail, instead of passing, under a sandbox that refuses a nested one unless `UNA_SKIP_NESTED_SANDBOX_TESTS=1` is set, a failure to put stderr back after `sandbox_init` still reports its reason, and `crates/una-sys/tests/solver_size.rs` caps the confinement files' size.
- **`Files` confinement denies a directory that exists but does not resolve** (#1380, `spec/14 §3` rule 2, ADR 0038 D9). When an intermediate directory of a path did not resolve and was not a symbolic link, confinement took it for a directory that does not exist and left the lexical path to the operation. A directory can also fail to resolve for another reason, such as a canonical path longer than the host's limit, and the operation then opened the lexical path without its directories having been checked against the prefix. The lexical path is now left to the operation only when a component does not exist or is not a directory, with the outcomes as before (`NotFound`, or `Io`); any other failure to resolve is `Denied`, in `una run` and in native executables. A new `World::absent` tells the cases apart, and its default denies. Tests reproduce the case on an in-memory model with a path limit, on the disk and in both engines, and check that the outcomes for directories that do not exist are unchanged.
- **Security: the timing label of a deadline counts what a reached handler runs** (#1436). The timing label of a `within`, a `first of`, a `for ever within`, a driven step and a `limiter` installation counted a handler that the code under the deadline can reach only by the deep labels of its parameters and by the operations its `uses` names, with `⊤` for a parameter of function type alone. A parameter that held a function inside a kind, a `List` or an `Option` counted as `public`, and the calls of function values, the calls of functions and the opaque applications in its operation bodies were not counted, so a function the handler was installed with could run a loop of `secret` length inside a `within` and `if error pass` published whether it timed out. A handler now counts by what its operation bodies run, their lambdas and tasks included, taken as the site's own code is, and a parameter whose type holds a function at any depth is `⊤`, whether or not the handler calls it (`spec/26 §2` rule 5 item 3, ADR 0116 IF5 item 3). `L_lim` gives the same `⊤` to such a parameter. A package that ran such a function under a deadline, or that installs a handler holding one where a deadline can reach it, is now rejected with U4001, U4002 or U4003. A value of a kind with a field of function type, given to a prelude function such as `fold` or `map` at a position other than a lambda written at the call, now counts as code not known at the call in a package without boundaries too (`⊤`), as it already did in one with boundaries. The same rule now decides what holds a function for the function values that cross a boundary (ADR 0093 rule 8): a value of a type parameter's type, or of a type not yet inferred, counts as one, so generic code that gives such a value to a boundary, or to a prelude function that applies a function argument, can be rejected where it was accepted before, in the safe direction. No example of the repository is affected.
- **Security: `--stop-after-iterations` and `stop after N` are a budget of each loop instance** (#1472, S-36, `spec/26 §6` rule 1, §8 rule 1, ADR 0116 rule 19 amendment draft `docs/plan/0116-s36-amendment-draft.md`): iterations were counted across every `for ever` of the run and their end was a sticky host stop, so a `for ever` under a `secret` branch moved where a later `public` loop stopped and its output revealed the secret; each entry into a `for ever`, and the driver's loop, now counts its own iterations from 0 and stops at its own iteration `N + 1`, in both engines and in native code, without interrupting operations or reaching other loops (edition `0.4` only; no released version has the flag). This closes the channel in the program's output and exit status only; the trace still carries a `stop` event after a secret-branch loop (S-39, #1507).
- **Security: the registry service bounds the work of a slow head and the memory of a declared body** (#1473). `una-itaty` searched a request's head for its end from the start after every read, so a peer that sent one byte at a time cost quadratic work; the search now resumes where it stopped. A body's buffer reserved its whole declared length (up to about 260 MiB for a publication) from the head alone, and a publication took its publishing slot only after its body was read, so many connections could exhaust memory with headers; the buffer now grows with the bytes that arrive, up to the declared length, and a publication takes its slot once its head is checked and before its body is read, so a third concurrent publication is refused with 503 before its body. A body now keeps at least 64 KiB per second on average, counted on the bytes that arrived, once its first idle timeout is past, so a peer that sends a byte now and then can no longer hold a publishing slot for the time its declared length alone would allow; the buffer takes its whole declared length at once only after its first MiB arrived, so growing it never holds more than that length and a MiB; a head whose end lies within its last bytes before the 16 KiB bound is answered the same however the peer splits its writes; and a request refused before its body gets its answer, then the service discards at most 64 KiB of the body for at most a second before it closes. The other limits, the strict parsing and the refusals are unchanged.
- **Security (S-39): code under a `secret` branch no longer shows in the trace through the events the runtime appends by itself** (#1507, `spec/26 §6` rule 4). A host stop, a budget, SIGTERM or `--deadline` that ended a `for ever` under a program counter above `public`, a `within` whose deadline cut its loop there, or a failing `tasks` statement that canceled its tasks there, in the code itself or in what it calls, a call of a `secret` function value included, appended a `stop`, `timeout` or `cancelled` event and moved the `seq` of every later event, so the number of events revealed the secret. Such code now appends none, and numbers its `tasks` statements and `first of` blocks apart, so it no longer moves the `task` paths of later public events, in both engines. The run-wide budget of `--stop-after-iterations` is S-36.
- **Security (S-40): a `start` under a `secret` branch no longer shows in task paths or `cancelled` events** (#1512, `spec/26 §6` rule 4, `spec/27 §7` rule 5). Inside a public `tasks` statement, a `start` under a program counter above `public` moved the index `k` of every later `start` of the same statement, so the `task` path `s<c>.<k>` of a later public task's events, and the worker that `k` picks, spelled out the secret, and when the block then failed it added one `cancelled` event per such task. Such a `start` is now quiet: its task, `s<c>.q`, takes no number and ends without its `cancelled` event, in both engines. Quiet code now numbers none of its `tasks` statements, `first of` blocks and `start`s (`qs`, `qf`, `q`, S-44, #1546), since code that is quiet without a `secret` branch, under a generic decision or an operation body, writes events whatever the secret, and a count in their paths showed how many quiet runs came before. Still open (#1545): a quiet task holds a slot of its statement's bound, so under `tasks at most n at once` it can make a later public `start` wait, which in the interpreter reorders public events, as a park inside quiet code does. Every task of one quiet `start` now runs on one worker, a cost of quiet code only.
- **Security (S-41): handler installations under a `secret` branch no longer move the instance ids of later public installations** (#1514, `spec/26 §6` rule 4). A `with` under a program counter above `public`, written there or in a callee, was counted by the run-wide count behind `with:<h>#<n>`, so the id of a later public installation of the same handler revealed the secret. Such installations, and every installation in quiet code, now take no number, `with:<h>#q`, in both engines and in every edition, so no instance id depends on the secret. Still open: the slots and parks of quiet tasks (#1545).
- **Security (S-42): operations performed under a `secret` branch no longer show in the trace by occurrence** (#1515, `spec/26 §6` rule 4, `spec/11 §5`). An operation of a capability that accepts a label above `public`, a handler's or a host's such as `Http` under `internal`, performed under a program counter above `public`, appended one event, its arguments redacted, so the number of events and the `seq` of every later event revealed the secret. An operation performed in quiet code now appends no event to a run's trace and takes no `seq`, in both engines and in every edition, wherever its handler is declared; an example's trace keeps it, for `expect performed`, and code quiet only by the timing floor of a `within` or `first of` keeps it, as `spec/27 §5` rule 7 says (Core marks it `quiet timing`). Code quiet only by the conservative rules (a generic decision, an operation body under an effect floor, a `via` body) loses its operations' events too, though they occur whatever the secret there. A failure that an iteration deadline causes in quiet code no longer counts as the iteration's first observation of it. Still open: the slots and parks of quiet tasks (#1545); the outcomes of later public operations on host state that hidden quiet code changed are closed by S-45 (#1552). The formal re-check does not cover declarations that hold `quiet`, which now include every operation under a raised program counter.
- **Security: a retired witness's cosignature counts offline only with a verified consistency proof** (#1529, ADR 0122 amendment of 2026-11-03, `spec/30 §4`, §9 rules 5, 7 and 9, §15). A provenance, now `ita-provenance/2`, carries the latest checkpoint signed under the last root version and the consistency proof that the lock's point is its prefix, and every load verifies them. Without them, as in a provenance of the earlier format, a point cosigned by a witness the last root retired is unavailable (U7013, exit code 1); `una ita fetch` stores the newer format beside the earlier one, which is kept. Every offline load, and `una ita unbundle`, reads the root-version floor Itaoca stores for the registry: a provenance whose root chain ends below it is refused (U7010), and a floor that cannot be read is unavailable (U7013). The lock's `registry` section records the last root version and its digest (`last root`), which a vendored chain must hold; a lock without the line is U7009, repaired by `una ita lock`. Past the stored floor, or with none, the vendored root chain is trusted on first use. The security hunt's Y3 controls carry the proof, with new forgeries of it and of the root chain.
- **Security: a task that starts past its turn's end still yields** (#1533, S-43, ADR 0117 rule 2; amendment draft `docs/plan/0117-seen-value-note-draft.md`): the end of a time slice that came before a task had installed its state could be lost, and then the task kept its worker while other ready tasks on that worker waited, a canceling task among them, beyond the bound of rule 2. The pending end now reaches the task's first check point (edition `0.4` only; no released version has the scheduler).
- **Hardening: an overdue turn's end is raised again** (#1535, follow-up of S-43 / #1533, ADR 0117 rule 2): under the real clock the slicer raised the end of a task's turn once, so the Fairness bound rested on that one raise being heard. While a turn stays past its end with another task waiting on its worker, the slicer now raises it again once per further slice, so a raise that is lost delays the waiting task by a slice instead of for good. A task alone on its worker is never interrupted (edition `0.4` only; no released version has the scheduler).
- **Security: the root-version floor binds the digest of its root metadata** (#1538, ADR 0122 amendment of 2026-11-09, L3). Itaoca stores the root-version floor of a registry as `<n> sha256:<hex>`, the version and the digest of that root metadata, still replaced only by a higher version. Every offline load and `una ita unbundle` refuse (U7010) a provenance or bundle whose root chain holds version `n` with another digest, a fork of the root chain, and the registry client refuses a walked chain the same way. A floor of the earlier form, the version alone, is still read and compared by version only; a floor file of neither form cannot be read and makes an offline load unavailable (U7013). Storing the floor stays a note on failure. Cost: O(R) more per load for R root versions.
- **Security: `una ita lock --check` verifies each registry package's provenance against the root version it accepted** (#1538, ADR 0122, amendment of 2026-11-09; `spec/30 §4`, §9 rule 7). With and without `--fresh`, the check now verifies the provenance of each registry package of the lock, the vendored copy or else Itaoca's, as the offline load does, against the larger of the root-version floor Itaoca stores and the last root version of the chain the command walked at the lock's point, whether or not storing that floor succeeded. A vendored provenance whose later root versions were dropped, with the lock's `last root` lowered to match, used to pass `--check` wherever the floor could not be stored, such as a CI run with an empty Itaoca; it is now refused (U7010) with the repair: lock again at a fresh resolution point. A retired witness counted without its proof, or a floor that cannot be read, is U7013. A lock whose provenance predates an honest root rotation now fails `--check` the same way, as it already failed `una check` once the floor was stored. Storing floors is still a note on failure.
- **Security: a same-size checkpoint under another root version is recorded as evidence** (#1538, `spec/30 §11` rule 9). When the registry client proves a lock's point that names an older root version a prefix of the latest checkpoint, and the latest checkpoint, signed under the last root version, is of the point's own size, the two are checkpoints of one size naming different versions: still refused (U7010), and now, when the log signed both, recorded as typed evidence against the registry with the two versions and the places read, and said so by `una ita lock --check`, `una ita fetch`, `una ita bundle` and `una ita unbundle`. A point the log did not sign is refused alone, with no evidence recorded.
- **The proof cache is off for a run when `.una`, `.una/cache` or `.una/cache/proofs` is a symbolic link** (#1540, the interim of `docs/plan/proof-cache-eviction.md` section 6.4). A link committed at any of the three, dangling or not, leading outside the package or inside it, makes `una` neither read nor write the cache through it; the run solves every query, so its statuses, counterexamples, diagnostics and exit code are those of a run with no cache, and only `obligations.cached` stays 0. Before, a store refused only a link leading outside the package, and reads followed every link. Removing the link brings the cache back.
- **Security: an operation served by a handler installed in a callee under a `secret` branch no longer shows in the trace** (#1544, `spec/26 §6` rule 4). A function with no effects of its own that installs a handler and performs an operation served by it, called under a program counter above `public`, appended that operation's event, so whether the event occurred revealed the secret. An operation served by an instance that quiet code installed, `with:<h>#q`, now appends no event and takes no `seq`, in both engines and in every edition. Where code is quiet only by the conservative rules (an effect floor, a decision on values of a type parameter), such operations lose their events: the safe direction. Since such an event is not recorded, the abort for an integer of more than 4300 digits that recording its arguments or result would raise (`spec/11 §7`) does not happen for it; this depends on where the handler was installed, not on the secret.
- **Security (S-45): a run's trace renders each operation's outcome at its label** (#1552, `spec/11 §5`, `spec/26 §6` rule 4, ADR 0032 amendment of 2026-11-06). The trace redacted an operation's `ok` value by its result's label but wrote whether it failed, its variant and its payload in clear, though the checker labels those decisions (ADR 0085, ADR 0094, `spec/27 §10`). So the outcomes of public `Files.read`s spelled out what hidden writes under an `internal` branch did, or the `internal` path of a public write, and a public `Limit.acquire` showed whether hidden code took the token, in both engines and every edition. In a run, an outcome whose label, the decision's joined with its failure type's and its result's, is above `public` is now `{"kind":"redacted","level":"internal"}`, with no text built from the value, in both engines and every edition; Core IR, `--dump-core`, `una --json core` (key `outcome`, additive in `una.core/1`) and the native description carry the label. Nothing stops compiling. The cost is observability: in a run's trace the outcomes of every `Http` request, `Files.read`, `Env.get`, `Keys.hmac`, `Limit.acquire`, `gives`-labeled operation and `Files` operation of a package whose file-state label is above `public` are redacted. An example's trace keeps its outcomes in clear. Readers of trace outcomes must accept the kind `redacted`.
- **Security S-46: whether `acquire` of `std.limit` fails is `internal`** (#1563, `spec/27 §10` rule 4, ADR 0117 IF5; edition `0.4`, unreleased). The checker did not apply the rule that `Limited` is labeled `internal`, so a program could branch on `attempt @gate.acquire()` and print the result: with a hidden `acquire` under an `internal` branch before it, it printed whether that branch took the token. Now whether a call of `Limit.acquire` fails, and its payload, carry `internal`, whatever handler serves it, so `attempt` gives an `internal` `Result`. **This rejects programs that compiled before:** a public print or result chosen by whether `acquire` failed is U4003 or U4001, and `if error pass` or `if error fail` of its failure is U4002; a parameter that receives the `Result` must be declared `internal Result[Unit, internal Limited]`. The diagnostics name the call: "the condition carries whether `@gate.acquire` failed, labeled `internal`, the label `Limited` carries" (`spec/13 §3`). A program that keeps what it decides at `internal`, handles the failure in place with `if error use`, or releases it through a policy of a trusted module still compiles. The conformance cases `runtime/limiter_acquire` and `runtime/limiter_tasks` now release their printed outcomes through a policy. The check is O(1) per operation site, so the checker stays linear.
- **Soundness: the timing label follows a policy call into its `via` function** (#1673, ADR 0116 IF5, IF6 and P1, `spec/07 §5`). A policy call runs its `via` function, but the timing label's call graph had no edge for it. So a deadline or a race around a policy call did not count what the `via` installs, and a request in a `via` called beside tasks escaped the label of a limited grant. Programs accepted before are now refused: a `within` or a `for ever within` around a policy whose `via` installs a handler with a `secret` parameter (U4001), and a policy whose `via` makes a request on a limited grant, called in a branch of `together`, the block of a `tasks` statement or a branch of `first of` (U4001 at the request).
- **Soundness: a deadline or race outcome reaches the result of a `via` function only with a public concurrent label** (#1673, ADR 0098 amendment of 2026-11-06, `spec/07 §5` rule 7). No label is checked in a `via` body, so a `within`, `first of` or `for ever within` there, or in code it calls, a lambda written there included, whose outcome became the policy's result released the timing of the tasks running beside the policy call. Each such site, and each statement a `limiter` installation scopes over, is now U4001 when its concurrent label, the preview's `τ_conc`, is above `public`: programs that call such a policy in a part of a task construct are refused, and the same policy called from sequential code stays accepted, unless the `via` calls a function value or applies one whose code is not known at the call: every lambda's code then counts as reached, so its timing sites are refused wherever the policy is called. One search of the call graph from every `via` function, O(V log F + E + A log A), then O(log n) per site, and a scan of the diagnostics so far for each site it refuses.

### Information flow and timing labels

- **Per-operation labels in edition `0.3`, part 1** (#566, ADR 0094). An operation in a header's `uses` brackets can carry `gives` and a label, `uses @service_files: Files[owner gives internal[Service]]`: through that binding the operation's answer, whether it failed and its payload carry the label, so the flow rules keep a service's answer from reaching the requester. Passing a labeled binding to a callee that reads the operation lower, by name or with `with`, is the new U3022, whose repairs never lower a label; rebindings and narrowings keep the labels; a function whose `uses` label an operation is not a value (U3006). `gives` is a contextual keyword of edition `0.3` (U0103 before it, U0101 in a function type, U7002 in a ceiling). Inferred labels of private functions, locked labels under `[contracts]` and the H5 premise in the build report follow in parts 2 and 3 (`docs/plan/0094-operation-labels-design.md`).
- **`Clock` readings carry their timing label** (#898, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-11-09, accepted, `spec/14 §3`), edition 0.4 preview. A reading of `Clock.now` is labeled with `τ⁰` of every ancestor of its site, joined with `τ_conc`, `L_res` and, in a driven package, the bodies of `init`, `step` and `on_stop`. Printing the elapsed time of work on `internal` or `secret` data is now U4003; a run on `public` data is unchanged, and an `internal` reading may be written with `Files.write`. The trace redacts a reading above `public` in both engines, and `una report` lists it under `information.timing` as `Clock.now`. A reading that a policy's `via` function reaches is U4001 when its label is above `public` (`spec/07 §5` rule 7), and a handler of `Clock` cannot forward the host's clock (U4001). Editions 0.1 to 0.3 do not change.
- **`Json` values carry labeled data in edition 0.4** (#917, ADR 0073 J5, amendment of 2026-11-09 accepted on 2026-11-10, `spec/23 §5` rule 2). A constructor of `Json` (`Boolean`, `Number`, `Decimal`, `Str`, `Array`, `Object`) accepts arguments at any label, and its result's top label is the join of every label of its arguments, so a server can echo `internal` request data as an `internal Json` in an `internal` reply, with the structure and escaping of `to_json` and no policy. Reading the value, `to_json`, equality and sinks are raised by that label, so the U4001 or U4003 moves from the constructor to where the labeled value reaches a lower declared type or sink. Editions 0.2 and 0.3 are unchanged.
- **A labeled `Json` names the constructor argument that labeled it** (#917, ADR 0073 J5, amendment of 2026-11-09 accepted on 2026-11-10). In edition 0.4, a U4001, U4002 or U4003 caused by a labeled `Json` value adds "the argument of `Str` made a `Json` value `secret`" to its message and shows that argument as a related location: the innermost, earliest labeled argument of a `Json` constructor in the declaration whose label the flow carries above what the sink allows.
- **A lambda written at the call counts by its captures in the timing label** (#1023, ADR 0116 IF5 item 4, `spec/26 §2` rule 5 and §7 rule 5). A call of `map`, `filter`, `first_where`, `fold`, the dense `map`, `zip_map`, `update` and `fold`, or the `update` of a map, whose every argument that may hold a function is a lambda written at the call for a parameter of function type no longer gives a driven step, a `within`, a `first of`, a `for ever within` or a `limiter` the top timing label: the lambda's captures and what its body runs count as code written outside it does. Any other function value, a parameter, a local, a field, a named function, a receiver holding functions or a lambda given to `fold`'s `initial`, keeps the top label, as do `L_res` and `L_lim`. The rule only makes the label less strict, and a test keeps its premise that no standard function keeps the argument of a function-typed parameter.
- **`for ever within` and the tick of a driven step take the label of the work beside them** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, C, slice 5 of the precise `τ_conc`, `spec/26 §2` rule 5 and §7 rule 5, `spec/27 §11`). A `for ever within` that a part of a task construct can reach now takes `κ` of the constructs around it, instead of `⊤`, and the tick's `input` needs `conc(step)`, the labels of the work beside every call of `step`: a step called beside tasks on `public` data needs no label on its tick.
- **`first of` and the residue label take the label of the work beside them** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, C and P9, slice 4 of the precise `τ_conc`, `spec/27 §6` rule 8 and §11). The timing label of a race that a part of a task construct can reach, and `L_res` of a `tasks` statement or a race that a part can reach, now take `κ` of the constructs around them instead of `⊤`: a race beside started tasks that handle only `public` data stays `public`, and a `tasks` statement that makes requests beside `public` work leaves `L_res` at `internal`. A race in a handler's operation that no part of a construct reaches is now `public` (P5).
- **The timing labels of nested sites are computed in near-linear time** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, E, slice 8 of the precise `τ_conc`). Each item a site's checking records goes once into the innermost open site, every site's label is joined bottom up from the summaries, and the reads of outer bindings are resolved by one sweep per package, so the labels of sites nested `d` deep cost O(d log d) instead of O(d²); a site that is reported still rebuilds its items to name the source of its label. No verdict or message changes.
- **The labels of concurrent work are computed** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, P2 to P4, slice 2 of the precise `τ_conc`). The checker computes `κ` of each task construct, `ctx` down each body's tree of constructs and `conc` of each node of the timing graph, once per package, in time linear in the graph, the constructs and the recorded seeds (which grow with the nesting of sites until slice 8 records each item once), and `τ_conc` of each timing site from them. No timing label uses them yet, so no program's outcome changes.
- **The timing label of a site comes from per-node summaries** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, E, slice 7 of the precise `τ_conc`). `τ⁰` of each `within`, `first of`, `for ever within` and `limiter` statement is now joined from summaries of every function, handler and operation, closed once per package, instead of a search of the call graph per site, so `n` sites that reach `n` functions take linear time instead of quadratic; the search runs only to name the source of a label that is reported. No verdict or message changes.
- **A `within` takes the label of the work that can run beside it** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, P0 to P5, slice 3 of the precise `τ_conc`, new `spec/27 §11`). The timing label of a `within` that a part of a task construct can reach is now `κ` of the constructs around it, the timing label of their parts, instead of `⊤`: a deadline beside started tasks or branches that handle only `public` data stays `public`, and one beside work on a `secret` is `secret`. A `within` in a lambda or in a handler's operation that no part reaches is now `public` (P5), where the preview gave it `⊤`. The other timing sites keep the preview's `⊤` until their slices.
- **The graph of the timing label records its call sites and task constructs** (#1087, ADR 0116 amendment of 2026-10-25, P1 to P3, slice 1 of the precise `τ_conc`). Each edge of the graph is recorded at its call site, with the node whose code holds it and the innermost task construct whose part holds it, and the constructs of each body form a tree. No timing label changes. A new test holds the graph to the Core IR of every package of the conformance suite.
- **Host premises in the build report** (#1395, part 3 of #566, ADR 0094 rule 5). `una report` gains the key `premises`, after `assumptions`: the premises on the host that a package's acceptance rests on, H1 to H4, the `Env` note and the new H5 (what an operation answers through a binding where it is labeled `l′`, and whether it fails, is a function only of the arguments and of the part of the host state at or below `l′`), each with its statement and what the package uses it for: the host operations its headers list that the premise covers (a package capability with a host capability's name does not count, and `tcb.host_capabilities` now tells them apart too), or, for H5, every operation a function's or a handler's header labels with `gives`. These premises are assumed of the deployment, never proven; a labeled operation is accepted on the checker's rules alone until the formal model covers labels (#563). The human report prints them under `assumed of the host:`. `spec/13 §6` states the key and the premises, and `docs/plan/formal-track-status.md` lists H5. The key is additive, and the schema stays `una.report/2`.
- **Inferred operation labels, locked labels and `gives` in `una contract`** (#1396, part 2 of #566, ADR 0094 rule 3 and ADR 0092 rule 8). In edition `0.3`, a private function without `uses` clauses now infers the labels of its operations: each reads at the join of the labels its callers' bindings give it, through `with @x = @y` and `with @x = @y[ops]`, so passing a labeled binding to it is no longer refused with U3022, and its body reads the answers at that label (a `public` return of such an answer is U4001, a branch on it raises the program counter). A private function with a declared header that lists the operation without `gives` still gets U3022, and a function whose inferred `uses` label an operation is not a function value (U3006). A `[contracts]` lock may now write `gives` and a label after a locked operation, instead of being refused with U7002: the header must read the operation at the lock's label or above, else U3023 at the operation, whose first repair raises the header's label to the join and which `una fix` applies; a category the package does not declare is U7002 at the entry. `una contract` prints an inferred header's labels, and `una.contract/1` gains `gives` in each `uses` entry that labels an operation, left out otherwise, so documents of editions `0.1` and `0.2` do not change. `spec/05 §3`, `§9` and `spec/13 §5`, `§7` state the rules.
- **Repairs that narrow before they widen at module boundaries** (#1397, part of #565, ADR 0093 rule 6, `spec/13 §3`, `§5`). In edition 0.3, a U3020 whose requirement lies within the boundary's ceiling, from a caller that holds a binding permitting it, has the grant `with @x = @y[ops]` narrowed to what the callee declares as its first repair, which `una fix` applies when `@y` is `@x` itself or the only binding that qualifies; when several qualify, the repair is advice that lists them, since which one the boundary receives is a decision about trust. At a function value and at a handler's installation the grant is advice; a requirement the ceiling withholds gets only advice for a handler within the ceiling, naming what it withholds. U3021's repair narrows the grant to the ceiling or says that no grant keeps both. U3001 and U3002 rank a narrowed rebinding of a binding the caller holds first, with an edit only when exactly one binding qualifies, and mark a widening of a public header as an authority change, last. `una fix` applies a boundary edit only as one narrowed U3020 grant, never an edit of U3021 or U3024, and never writes a ceiling. Editions 0.1 and 0.2 keep their repairs.
- **Flow reports name the labeled answer they reveal** (#1465, ADR 0094 rule 7). In edition `0.3`, a U4001, U4002 or U4003 whose label comes from the answer of an operation that its binding labels with `gives` now names that binding and operation: the message ends with "; this value carries the answer of `@service_files.owner`, labeled `internal[Service]`" ("the condition carries" for a flow through the program counter, "this flow carries" at a parameter of a function type), the call is a related location, and the first repair says to read the operation through a binding whose answers are within the label allowed or to send the value (or run the code) elsewhere; a diagnostic without an escape repair gains one, last, to release the answer through a policy of a trusted module. The answer named is the first in source order whose label the reported label carries and the allowed label does not. Codes, locations and verdicts do not change, and packages that label no operation, every package of editions `0.1` and `0.2` among them, get the same messages as before. `spec/13 §3` states the rule.
- **`una core` exports operation labels, and the formal re-check no longer accepts a labeled operation** (#1518, ADR 0094 rule 8, `spec/13 §8`). A `uses` entry of `una.core/1` holds `gives`, after `ops`, for each operation its binding labels, declared or inferred, left out when none is labeled, so documents of packages without labels are unchanged. The re-check reports a declaration whose `uses` label an operation, a handler's operation among them, and what reaches it, as not re-checked, "F1 restriction (a labeled operation, ADR 0094 rule 8)", until F1 models operation labels (#563): before, the export wrote no label and such a declaration could be reported accepted under H1 to H3, without the premise H5 it rests on.
- **Conformance and spec gaps of the ADR 0093 and ADR 0094 audit** (#1519). Two cases pin what only an explain example or the shared parser branch covered: `security/boundary_release_direct`, a boundary function that calls the release in its own body (U3024), and `syntax/gives_in_0_1` with its erasure twin, `gives` in a header's `uses` in edition 0.1 (U0103). `spec/07 §3` rule 1 points to the operation-label rule in §7 rule 4, and `spec/02` notes that the grammar ADR 0093 and ADR 0094 list as §4 and §3 lives in §6 and §2.
- **The formal re-check reads `quiet e` as `e`** (#1553, S-39, S-42, `spec/26 §6` rule 4). The F1 importer no longer reports a declaration that holds Core's `quiet` as not re-checked: what `quiet` wraps is read and re-checked as any other code, so the operations and calls under a raised program counter that S-42 marks are covered again (relative to F1's trace; the step to the runtime's trace, which leaves their events out, is argued, not proven).
- **Tests and spec wording of the S-40, S-42 and S-45 follow-ups** (#1583, `spec/26 §6` rule 4, `spec/27 §5` rule 7, `spec/11 §5`). The deadline tests of `http.rs` check the variants the native executable writes, a native operation description without its `outcome` key is tested to fail to load, the conformance case `outcomes_at_their_labels` is run for whole traces in both engines, the checker's closure of `quiet` calls has a scaling test, and three spec passages are corrected; no behavior change.
- **A request task that can recurse and hold `secret` data is refused** (#1674, ADR 0123 IF6, `spec/31 §1` rule 5 item 6). A started task past `--task-depth` ends alone and is answered `500`, so its status told a peer how deep it recursed, as in a probe that recovered a secret integer by the length of the request body; a `start` that moves a reply right is now U4003 when its task can recurse, call a function value or reach code the checker does not see, and can hold data above `internal`, a coarse rule computed once per package in linear time. The default of `if error use` now runs under the label that decides the failure, so its effects and calls are checked under it.
- **Spec and tests: the depth of a started task as a channel** (#1677, `spec/27 §8` rule 5). The rule of #1684 already refuses a recursion that data above `internal` decide in a program that starts tasks, so whether a task ends alone past `--task-depth`, and so whether its later effects happen, can follow `internal` data at most; that residual is now stated and pinned by `security/task_depth_internal` in both engines, beside #1708's, and `security/short_circuit_effect` pins that a call with effects cannot stand in the right operand of `and`, `or` or `implies`.
- **Spec and tests: a lowered `--task-depth` against the static depth of a request task** (#1681, `spec/31 §1`). A request task with no recursion ends alone only when an operator sets `--task-depth` below the longest chain its code shows, and which chain runs is a branch, which data above `internal` cannot decide in a program that starts tasks (`spec/27 §8` rule 5, #1684); `spec/31 §1` now says so, while that rule stands, instead of calling the case uncovered, and `security/server_start_static_depth_secret` with its `internal` twin pins it.
- **IF6's depth rule refuses only tasks whose calls labeled data decide** (#1682, `spec/31 §1`, ADR 0123 IF6). A request task that can recurse and holds data above `internal` is now refused only when data above `internal` decide one of the calls it can make, when every call it can make lies in code the checker records with its labels; with a function value, a handler's operation, a `via` or unseen code in its reach, the coarse test of #1674 stays. A task that recurses on the request's length and holds a `secret` key that decides none of its calls is accepted, live in both engines.
- **A check point that `secret` data decide is refused in a program that starts tasks** (#1684, `spec/27 §8` rule 5). The interpreter and native `--virtual-time` switch tasks after 1024 check points, so a started task whose pure loop ran as long as a `secret` bit decided reordered later public prints and spelled the key, one bit per task; in a package with a `tasks` statement or a `serve` section, a call, an operation, a loop, a `map`, `filter`, `first_where` or `fold`, a call of a function value, a generic call whose function argument returns such data, or a `tasks at most` bound that data above `internal` decide is now U4002, a coarse rule computed in near linear time, and the right operand of `and`, `or` and `implies` is checked under the label of the left one; data at `internal` still can order those effects (#1708).
- **The timing label counts the values that the code a site reaches computes** (#1685, item 7 of ADR 0116 IF5, by its amendment of 2026-11-06, and `spec/26 §7` rule 5 and §2 rule 5). `τ` of a `within`, a `first of`, a `for ever within`, a driven step and the statement a `limiter` scopes over counted the bindings a site reads from outside and the operations it reaches, never a value that a called function or a handler's operation labels from its own literals, so `let k: secret Int = 3000000` one call away gave a `public` `τ` while passing the same `k` in was refused. Every value checked now joins `ℓ*` of its type, type parameters' labels left out, into its function's or handler's body label and the open sites' seeds; each site joins the body labels of what it reaches, as does the call graph of ADR 0116 P1. A reached function that computes on a parameter labeled above its argument now counts at the parameter's label, so this refuses more than IF5's six items did. Linear in the size of the bodies.
- **The task-switch rule leaves out code no run reaches** (#1707, `spec/27 §8` rule 5). A check point that data above `internal` decide is refused only in code a run of the program can reach from its `pub` functions, predicates, policies, `via`s, function values, handlers, lambdas and code outside functions; a private function that only `test` declarations call, which `una test` runs and a run never does, is no longer refused.
- **The report lists the check points that `internal` data decide in a program that starts tasks** (#1708, `spec/27 §8` rule 5, `spec/13 §6`). Their count can still order the public effects of started tasks under the interpreter and native `--virtual-time`, the open residual of #1684; `una report` now names each one in `information.timing` as a `task switch` at `internal`, and nothing that was accepted is refused.
- **The timing rule of `via` follows function values precisely when none can come from outside** (#1710, `spec/07 §5` rule 7). A `via` that calls or applies a function value now counts only the lambdas written in the code it reaches and the functions it names as values, unless a value can come from outside that code (a function-holding parameter of the `via`, an operation answer, a handler's parameters or value calls, an unchecked function); only then does every lambda count, so a `within` in an unrelated lambda is no longer refused wherever such a policy is called.

### Loops, stops and driven programs

- **`stop` and `skip` in `for` loops, edition 0.4 preview** (#940, ADR 0116 rule 6, `spec/26 §3`). `stop` ends the innermost loop, `stop when c` does so when `c` holds, and `skip` goes on to the next iteration, each with the loop's state as it stands. They are U2034 outside a loop body or when their loop lies outside the nearest task. A statement after an unconditional `stop` or `skip` in the same block never runs and is U2040, whose repair removes it. Whether a loop stops may not depend on data its body did not start with (U4002), and the rest of a body after a `skip` under a labeled branch runs under that branch's label. Core IR gains `exit_k(b, ...)`, with the tag `next` or `stop`, which both engines and the native backend run, and which `una core --json` exports as the node kind `exit`.
- **`for until c decreases m`, edition 0.4 preview** (#941, ADR 0116 rule 7, `spec/26 §4`). The loop ends at the start of an iteration where `c` holds; `m`, an integer in the refinement language that is never run, must stay at least 0 where the loop continues (`decreases_bound`) and decrease in every iteration that goes on (`decreases_step`), so the verifier proves that the loop ends. `not c` holds in the body, and `c` after a loop without `stop`. The body runs under the label of `c`. Without `decreases` the loop is U0101. Core IR gains the loop source `until`.
- **`diverges`, `for ever` and `on stop`, edition 0.4 preview** (#942, ADR 0116 rules 8 to 10, `spec/26 §5`). A function that may run forever says `diverges` in its header, which `una contract` reports; `for ever` and calls of such functions are legal only in its body, outside lambdas, `together` tasks, `on stop` blocks and `for ever` bodies (U3027), and such a function is not a value (U3028). `for ever` runs until `stop` or a failure; its `on stop` block runs once the loop has ended, after a failure with the state from before the loop, and must not fail (U3029). Core IR gains the loop source `ever` and the loop's `on_stop` block.
- **Host stops, edition 0.4 preview** (#943, ADR 0116 rules 12 to 19 and 21, `spec/26 §6`). SIGINT, SIGTERM, `--deadline <seconds>` and `--stop-after-iterations <n>` are host stop requests of `una run` and executables, sticky for the rest of the run: every `for ever`, the driver's loop included, ends at its next iteration start with a `stop` event in the trace and runs its `on stop`, and the run then ends normally. An `Http` operation in the body of a `for ever` that waits, or starts, after a request fails with the new variant `HttpError.Interrupted` (edition 0.4 only). The hard stop comes at the end of `--stop-grace <seconds>` (default 10) or at a second request and cancels the run at its next call or loop iteration with U6007, exit code 1; a third request, or a second grace period after the hard stop, ends the process with SIGTERM. Signal handlers are installed only for runs whose `main` says `diverges` or that run a driver, in the new `una-sys` crate. The report's `out_of_model` list gains "the time of a host stop", "interference of the host's load with the timing of an expression" and "uncatchable termination".
- **`for ever within d`, edition 0.4 preview** (#944, ADR 0116 rule 11, IF5 to IF8, rule 21, `spec/26 §7`). Each iteration of the loop has the deadline `d`, an `Int of Millisecond` of at least 1 (U2028, U2001, U5001), after its start; past it, every `Http` operation the iteration waits on or starts fails at once with `TimedOut`, in both engines, and the trace gets one `timeout` event per iteration, with the loop's own task, where the iteration first observes the failure.
