The budget will stop at U6003 at the block instead of being exceeded, and the native case of #314, whose site varied with how threads were scheduled, will stop at one site. Native reference counts and copy marks will share one 64-bit word, 32 bits each, so no heap slot grows; a retain past 4294967295 references to one value, or a copy of more than 4294967295 objects, will stop the run. The interpreter does not change.
- **A native reference count is 32 bits, and a retain at its limit ends the process** (#777, ADR 0113 step 1). A retain past 4294967295 references to one value stops with one line on stderr: U6003 when the run holds the memory that many references take, else U8001 as an internal error. No count ever wraps, and no heap slot grows when the copies of a `together` block gain their marks.
- **The three runtime tests that Miri did not finish now run under it, at smaller sizes** (#778). Each allocation is slow under Miri, and the three were large, not stuck: a copy between heaps that also builds a 200,000-deep chain, 10,000 rounds of `find`, and more than 100,000 calls of the entry points. Under Miri they now run at depth 1,000, 200 rounds, and one word of each kind, and their full sizes still run in the ordinary tests. `scripts/sanitize.sh` pins the sizes, and the tests Miri skips drop from 26 to 23. The `sanitize` gate also runs the unit tests of the runtime at their full sizes under AddressSanitizer, after checking that they are instrumented and that AddressSanitizer is active.
- **TLS dependency updated for four advisories** (#779). `Cargo.lock` moves rustls from 0.23.45 to 0.23.46, the release that fixes RUSTSEC-2026-0346, RUSTSEC-2026-0347, RUSTSEC-2026-0348 and RUSTSEC-2026-0349. rustls is the TLS library of the `Http` capability in both engines. No other dependency changes.
- **The `site` gate audits the website's npm packages** (#783). Before `npm ci`, `scripts/site-website.sh` runs the pinned website's `scripts/deps-audit.mjs`: `npm audit` of its `package-lock.json` against the npm registry, with npm at the version the website pins, failing on any advisory, on an audit that does not answer, offline included, and on one that did not count every package. The website's tests, which its build runs, check the lockfile against its supply-chain review record, every package by path, version, license and integrity hash, from the registry only, with licenses from an allowlist: permissive ones, and LGPL-3.0-or-later only for the build-time image tool `sharp` and its `@img` packages. `site/website.lock` moves to the website commit that adds both.
- **The release gate builds the website with Learn's translations** (#789). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at a816a764, which publishes a page of Una by Example in Brazilian Portuguese or Spanish only when its translation is approved for the pinned English text, and pins una-lang/una-by-example at 6483a62c, whose `scripts/check.py` checks the translations.
- **The `hunt` release gate compares the checker with the formal model's re-check** (#791). Every program of the gate's fixed run that `una check` accepts is exported as Core and read by the model's checker, `una-core-check`, which the gate builds with the pinned Lean after the `formal` gate; a declaration the re-check rejects fails the gate. A program counts as compared only when the re-check accepts one of its own functions, with floors on compared programs and accepted declarations; what the re-check does not read, whole-document fallbacks and declarations it does not re-check, is pinned per reason, the expected `unknown` outcomes by name, and the residual comparisons by count, so a silent change fails; planted Core documents the re-check must reject, with a control it must read clean, show that it can see a fault. It found S-27 (#799) on its first run.
- **The `hunt` release gate compares the native backend with the interpreter on a sample of its programs** (#792). About 5% of the oracle's programs, a fixed sample, are built with `una build` and run natively, every variant. Their exit code, output, failure and trace must equal the interpreter's (`spec/16 §5`), by the rules of the native equivalence test, the ones for tasks and resource aborts included. A difference is a backend divergence and fails the gate. A sampled program the native backend does not compile must be pinned by name, and a canary checks that the comparison reports a planted divergence.
- **The Core export labels values computed from a labeled value with that value's label** (#799, S-27). In edition 0.2 and later, interpolating a value writes its fields without redaction, and the checker labels the text with every label of the value's type, its fields' included; the export bound that text to a temporary labeled without the labels that kinds' fields declare, so a record with a `secret` field was exported as `public` text holding the field in clear. A `when` over a literal or a range compares the scrutinee, and the export labeled those comparisons `public` whatever the scrutinee's label. The formal re-check rejected such programs, rightly. The export now carries the checker's labels. No program's verdict, output or trace changes: the checker, the redaction of traces and the arguments of operations were already right.
- **A text literal with interpolation in a pattern is U4008, in every edition** (#801, S-28). A `when` arm `is "…"` and the expression `x is "…"` treated their literal as a constant, `public`, but a text literal with interpolation is computed from the values it interpolates. So a `when` could choose the arm that prints, and an `is` give a `public` `Bool`, by a `secret` value, with no diagnostic. A pattern's literal is now a constant: interpolating in it is U4008, whose explanation shows the repair, comparing with `==`, whose result carries the value's label. Every release up to and including v0.3.14 and v0.1.10 accepts such programs (those two confirmed by running): the parser and the checker have read a pattern's text this way since v0.1.0. This is a soundness fix, so it applies to editions 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 alike, and a program it now rejects was leaking.
- **The `hunt` release gate's oracle covers `together`** (#807). A new operator of family C gives the two-run oracle programs where `together` could let a secret decide what an observer sees: tasks over secret elements, a secret branch, secrets sent by tasks, a block under a public condition, and a fan-out whose size the secret decides. Their public output must not move with the secret, and the native sample now compares task runs of generated programs too. A form the checks reject is kept as a must-reject probe. A lexical or syntax code on a generated program, or a must-reject program rejected without its expected code, now fails the gate as a generator's error, with a canary.

## v0.3.14

A patch of the Beta, with a fix to the boundary rules of edition `0.3`. A handler installed outside a call into a `[boundaries]` extent and granted on to it by name is now charged where it is installed: its `uses` and what its installation grants lie within the boundary's ceiling (U3021), and a release it reaches is rejected (U3024). This closes the known limitation of v0.3.13 (#669, ADR 0093 rule 15). A policy whose `via` function only signs with a literal key through `Keys.hmac` now fails at the program counter rather than at its `to` label, in editions `0.1` to `0.3` (`spec/07 §5` rule 6, `spec/10 §13.4`, #497, ADR 0098), with its formal statement merged (#587). This accepts some programs that v0.3.13 rejected (ADR 0090); the shape is narrow and checked fail-closed. In both backends, `slice` outside its text and `replace` with an empty pattern abort at the call with U8001, where native code clamped them. `spec/14` states what the prelude's functions compute, and `spec/11` how a closure keeps the capabilities where it is made. The release archives are reproducible, owned by `0:0` with no names, and a release gate rebuilds them and compares their digests. The specification and guides use neutral wording, and the website that the release gate builds gains reference pages for the standard library and its built-in methods. Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.14-validation.md`.

- **The Linux runs of `scripts/local-ci.sh` size their build to the VM's memory** (#701). The containers built with one job per CPU of the Docker VM, which could run it out of memory and kill the linker before any test ran. The build now runs one job per 2 GiB of the VM's memory, at most one per CPU. A `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` set on the host can lower that count but not raise it, and a `RUST_TEST_THREADS` set on the host is passed in. The run prints the job count and the container's peak memory (`docs/releases/validation.md`).
- **Neutral attributions in code and test comments** (#688). Comments under `crates/` and `tests/` no longer name a person or cite an internal ruling number. Each keeps its date, ADR, issue and review finding. Only comment lines change.
- **`slice` and `replace` abort outside their preconditions in both backends** (#693, `spec/22 §2`). A checked program cannot call them there, but if one did, the two backends behaved differently:
  - the interpreter stopped only on a negative `slice` position;
  - native code mapped such a position to 0;
  - both clamped an end past the text, and replaced an empty `replace` pattern.

  Now `slice` outside `0 <= start <= end <= t.len`, and `replace` with an empty pattern, abort at the call with U8001 and exit code 3, with the same message in both backends, as `set` does (#480).
- **`spec/14` states what the prelude's functions compute** (#692). Beyond their contracts, `spec/14 §1` now gives:
  - the element that `first` and `at` return, and where `append` puts the item;
  - the order of `map` and `filter`, and that `first_where` returns the first accepted element and calls `keep` no further;
  - the accumulator of `fold`, from first to last;
  - that `sum` is exact and `0` on the empty list in every edition;
  - `or_else`, whose `default` is evaluated before the call;
  - the texts for which `to_int` is `None`, and that it never overflows;
  - the meaning of `Nat`, `Option` and `Result`.

  These are what both backends already do. New runtime cases pin them in both backends; `or_else`'s `default` needs no pin, since an argument with effects is bound with `let` first (U3013).
- **Reproducible release archives** (#679). Two builds of one commit gave archives with different digests, so a published archive could not be rebuilt and checked against its validation record. The binaries were already reproducible. The archive differed by the time of each tar entry, the account and group that owned each entry, and the gzip header's time. Each entry now takes the commit's time (`SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, else the commit's own), is owned by `0:0` with no user or group name, and has a fixed mode; the entries go in byte order, and gzip stores no time. `scripts/check-release.sh` checks the owners, and a new `reproducible` release gate builds the archives a second time from cold and compares their digests (`docs/releases/validation.md`).
- **A handler granted on by name to a call into a boundary is charged where it is installed** (#669, ADR 0093 rule 15, `spec/05 §4` rule 5, `spec/07 §6`). In edition `0.3`, a handler installed outside a call into a boundary and granted on to it by name, through header bindings, rebindings and calls, counted at the call as its capability and operations only: its own `uses` were not counted against the ceiling, and the releases it reaches were not counted for U3024. Such a handler is now checked where it is installed, by `with` or by an example's `given`: U3021 when its `uses`, or what its installation's arguments grant, exceed the ceiling, and U3024 when it reaches a release, each with the call as the related span. The handlers that its own `uses` resolve to are granted on as well. This closes the known limitation of v0.3.13.
- **`spec/11` states that a closure keeps the capabilities where it is made** (#674). A closure value is ⟨λ, ρ, H⟩, and two rules join §4: E-Lam makes it with the capability environment in scope, and E-App runs its body under that environment, whoever calls it. This is the semantics the interpreter and the native backend already implement, and the one that `spec/04 §6` rule 3 and the reference check of `spec/05 §6` rule 7 rely on. The citations in the checker and the native backend name the rules.
- **The formal model reads `Keys.hmac`** (#587). F1 models a `Keys.hmac` call with a ghost event that no observer sees, and reads `Hashed` as a text at its label. T-Hmac asks for a `public` key name and program counter and labels the result `internal` joined with the data's labels; unary soundness and T6 cover it. The re-check reads `Keys.hmac` calls, except in a document that declares a handler of `Keys`, or of a capability it does not declare, where a handler may run in their place: such a call is not read (fail closed). A `via` function that calls `Keys.hmac` stays outside B1, so what calls its policy is not re-checked. 425 theorems, no `sorry`; the replay unchanged; corpus: 4 declarations from not re-checked to accepted (functions that call `Keys.hmac`), none rejected.
- **ADR 0098's `via` shape in the formal model** (#587, #497). A `via` function whose only failure points are top-level `Keys.hmac` calls with a literal key name, their failure passed on, records only ghost events and fails with the same code in any two environments. B1 admits it, and its policy's failure is labeled at the program counter; unary soundness and T6 hold for it. An example shows two secrets failing alike without the key and succeeding alike with it. A `via` function that handles the failure with `if error fail` is not of the shape yet, so what calls its policy is not re-checked. 433 theorems, no `sorry`; the replay unchanged; corpus: no change beyond the first commit's, none rejected.
- **A guide page for `[boundaries]`** (#662). `docs/guide/configure/boundaries.md` describes the `una.toml` table of edition `0.3`: the ceiling of each boundary, calls into a boundary and what they grant (U3020, U3021, U3024), the invalid tables (U7002), and the known limitation of a handler granted on by name. Its examples are checked by the guide test with their expected outcomes.
- **The website gains a configuration page for `[boundaries]`** (#685). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that lists `/docs/configure/boundaries/`, built from `docs/guide/configure/boundaries.md` (#662), in the docs tree and on the configure group page. The schemas index also checks that each row of the agent guide's table of commands has two cells, and lists each unpublished schema once (website#6).
- **Neutral wording in the published specification and guides** (#647, ADR 0088). `spec/11`, `spec/16` and `spec/18` drop a person's name from the attribution of three amendments, keeping their dates and ADRs. Every spec chapter, the agent guide and `una explain U6004` drop internal ruling numbers; ADRs, issue numbers and identifiers of review findings stay. The rules do not change.
- **A generated listing of the standard library** (#648). `docs/std/listing.json` lists every public item of the prelude and of `std.testing`, as the checker reads them in each edition, with one variant per run of editions where its declaration differs, its doc comments and the spec sections they cite. `una_check::standard_source` exposes the source the checker loads, read-only. Every prelude and `std.testing` item now has a doc comment that cites the specification. A test keeps the listing current, requires the doc comments and their references, and checks each item's editions against `una check`; another checks that each of edition 0.2's rewrites of the prelude applies exactly once, so an edit of the prelude cannot turn one off silently. The schema, `una.std-listing/1`, is unstable and not part of the specification (`docs/std/README.md`); the site bundle carries it. Built-in operations are not listed (#690).
- **The website gains the standard library's item pages** (#696). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that builds `/docs/std/prelude/<item>/` and `/docs/std/testing/<item>/`, one page per item of `docs/std/listing.json` (#648), with an index per module that says built-in operations are not listed (#690). The website's build now requires a docs bundle, and publishes spec chapters 05, 11, 16 and 18 only from a bundle of una 0.3.14 or later, the first release where they are neutral at the source (#647) (website#6, website#23).
- **The standard modules' doc comments hold no raw HTML, lists or fences** (#695). `crates/una-cli/tests/std_listing.rs` fails when a doc comment of a prelude or `std.testing` item holds `<` or `&` outside a code span, or a line that starts a list item or a code fence, which the website would render as HTML or join into one paragraph. Today's comments pass, so `docs/std/listing.json` does not change.
- **The standard library reference lists the built-in methods** (#690). `docs/std/builtins.json` lists `.len`, the methods of maps, the conversions and the wrapping, saturating and bit operations of fixed-width integers, with a signature line per receiver, the editions that have it, and a doc comment that paraphrases and cites `spec/14 §2`, `spec/18 §4`, `§5` and `spec/21 §2`, under the unstable schema `una.std-builtins/1`. `una_check::builtin_methods` lists, read-only, the methods the checker resolves by receiver, from its own tables; the methods of maps now come from one table, `MAP_METHODS`, with the same diagnostics. A test keeps the reference and the checker in step both ways, and probes each written signature in every edition with a typed call that must check and a wrongly typed one that must not.
- **A policy whose `via` function only signs with a literal key fails at the program counter** (#497, ADR 0098). ADR 0090 labels a policy call's failure with its `to` label, which also rejected the documented `Keys.hmac` pattern, where the failure depends only on the key name: `examples/events` and the declassification tour had to work around it (#496). A `via` function whose only failure points are top-level `Keys.hmac` calls with a plain literal key name, handled by `if error pass` or `if error fail` without a payload, in a package that installs no `Keys` handler outside examples, now fails at the program counter; every other `via` keeps ADR 0090's label, and the check fails closed. The workarounds are reverted. Formal statement: #587 (`spec/10 §13.4`).
- **The website gains the built-in method pages and the translation review** (#706). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that builds `/docs/std/builtin/` and a page per built-in method from `docs/std/builtins.json` (#690), and that checks the translation review on every build: the draft glossaries, the review notes and `i18n/REVIEW.md`, in which no string is approved yet (website#6, website#7).

## v0.3.13

A patch of the Beta, with a security fix in `una`. In every edition, a function named as a value was checked against the handlers in scope where the value was called but ran on those in scope where it was named, and a reference with no binding of a name the function uses was accepted and stopped the run, or failed `una build` with an internal error; such a reference now needs the function's `uses` where it is written (U3001 or U3003). Every earlier release is affected; the `0.1` release line receives the same fix. In edition `0.3`, a call into a module of a `[boundaries]` extent from outside it takes no capability by name: each capability the callee needs is granted with `with`, what the call grants, with the handlers that its lambdas and function values run on, lies within the boundary's ceiling, and a call into the boundary from which a release is reachable is rejected (ADR 0093, with U3020, U3021 and U3024 explained by `una explain`); these boundary rules do not apply to editions `0.1` and `0.2`. A handler installed outside the crossing statement and granted on to the boundary as a header capability is a known limitation: its `uses` and releases are not yet charged at the crossing (#669). The released sections of the changelog are checked against their tags. The website that the release gate builds gains a page per JSON schema, an editor page and a releases page. Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.13-validation.md`.

- **The website's schemas index takes its unpublished schemas from the agent guide** (#658). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit whose schemas index lists each schema that the agent guide's table of commands names but no published chapter defines, `una.build/1` of `spec/16` at this release, with the commands that print it. The website's build fails when a row of that table names a version that the specification has replaced, and a schema page no longer copies a table or list next to a code block whole (website#6).
- **The website gains a page per JSON schema, an editor page and a releases page** (#655). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that generates one page per JSON schema that a published spec chapter defines (12 at this release, `una.trace/1` among them), each from what the specification says about it, and fails its build when a schema is named but neither it nor a later version is defined. It also adds `/docs/editor/` on `una lsp`, and a `/releases/` page for the current release, whose Download and Releases pages say that no archive is listed yet before the release's validation record exists (website#6).
- **The tutorial no longer points to a missing editor extension file** (#651). `docs/guide/getting-started.md` §7 named `images/una-language-support-0.2.0.vsix`, which the repository does not hold, and an older version of the extension; the section now describes `una lsp` only. `crates/una-cli/tests/guide.rs` gains `every_repository_path_in_the_guides_exists`: a path written in inline code in `docs/guide/`, whose first segment is a top-level directory of the repository, such as `images/...` or `examples/...`, must name a file or directory. Markdown links were already checked by the site gate, inline code was not.
- **Neutral wording in the plan documents** (#650, ADR 0088). The documents under `docs/plan/` state their decisions, approvals and reviews, with their dates and scope, without naming who made them, and drop internal ruling numbers. Identifiers of review findings stay.
- **Released sections of the changelog are checked against their tags** (#645). `scripts/check-released-sections.sh` fails when a `## vX` section of `CHANGELOG.md`, for each tag `vX` from `v0.3.0` on, is not byte-identical to that section at the tag, and names each version that differs. A rebase that carries a new entry under a released heading, instead of `## Unreleased`, now fails before it merges. A commit that predates one of those tags fails until it is rebased over it, except in `scripts/release-gates.sh`, which checks only the tags the gated commit contains, so a past release can be gated again. A repository without those tags, such as a shallow clone, fails with how to fetch them, naming the repository it was cloned from; `UNA_SKIP_RELEASED_SECTIONS`, set to a reason, skips the check visibly, but never in `scripts/local-ci.sh` or `scripts/release-gates.sh`, whose evidence does not take it from the caller's shell. A shallow repository fails too. `scripts/local-ci.sh` runs it as a step; with `--linux` and `--linux-arm64` it runs on the host before the container, which skips it. CI's checkout fetches the whole history for it.
- **`CLAUDE.md` states the Linux exception of the validation rules** (#663). Its Validation section now says, as `docs/releases/validation.md` §1 rule 1 has since #605, that a pull request that changes what the Linux run runs also needs a `scripts/local-ci.sh --linux-arm64` PASS on its exact tip.
- **The plan documents name no author or internal id** (#650). `docs/plan/formal-track.md` and `docs/plan/formal-track-453-phase-b.md` drop their `Author:` lines, and `docs/plan/backlog.md` cites four findings as "v0.1 re-review" with the date of their decision, the issue link being the reference, instead of by internal ids.
- **Module boundaries, part 2: calls into a boundary** (#565, ADR 0093 with its amendments). In edition `0.3`, a call into a module of a `[boundaries]` extent, from outside it, takes no capability by name: each one the callee needs is granted with `with` (U3020), and what the call grants, each binding at every operation it permits, handlers with their own `uses` and the functional arguments' `uses`, lies within the ceiling (U3021). Function values, handler installations, policy calls and examples are calls too; a handler of a boundary installed with `with` takes its `uses` from the other bindings of the same `with`. A call from which a release is reachable is U3024. A function value given to a boundary whose code is not known at the call, from a parameter, a local, a computed value or a kind's field, is U3024 too when any policy is declared, and a forwarded `uses of` parameter is U3021: both fail closed. Inference never takes a capability across a boundary. An example outside a boundary can call into it only for functions that need no capability (#642). A handler installed outside the crossing statement and granted on to the boundary as a header capability is a known limitation: its `uses` and releases are not yet charged at the crossing (#669). A malformed or unknown ceiling entry permits nothing, so an edition `0.3` package with one now also gets U3021 at each call that grants that boundary a capability, besides the entry's U7002. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged.
- **Security: a function value runs on the bindings where it is written, and the checker now says so** (#565, ADR 0093 amendment of 2026-10-14, `spec/05 §4` and `§6`, `spec/07 §6`). A lambda keeps the capabilities in scope where it is written, and a function named as a value those where it is named (`spec/11 §4`). For a named function the checker took its `uses` only from the code that called the value: in every edition, a reference with no binding of a name its function uses was accepted and stopped the run (U8001), and a reference under one handler that was called under another was checked against the second and ran on the first. A reference now needs the function's `uses` where it is written, U3001 or U3003 there, as a lambda's definition does; the call of the value still needs its type's `uses`. In edition `0.3`, a boundary that called a lambda or function value under a handler of its own ran the caller's handler, whose `uses` and releases the ceiling and U3024 did not count: they now count at the call into the boundary, as do those of the lambdas and functions given to a handler's installation when the call is granted that handler, and a binding captured by a lambda written outside the call, which the check does not follow, makes the value unknown (U3021, and U3024 when a policy is declared). So does a function value from a parameter, a local or a computed value whose type has `uses`, given to a boundary or to a handler installed for a call into one: it runs on bindings captured where it was made, so it is U3021 at the call even when no policy is declared.

## v0.3.12

A patch of the Beta, with no security fix in `una` and no change to the language, its editions or its diagnostics. Each package under `examples/` gains a `README.md` that says what the program does, what each file shows, what `una check` proves for it, its edition, and the commands to check, test, run and build it, with what they print; a test runs those commands in a fresh copy of each package. The website that the release gate builds renders each README as the prose of its example page, above the files, and fails its build when a code fence on a page is closed early or never. Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.12-validation.md`.

- **A README for each example** (#586). Each package under `examples/` (`hello`, `wordfreq`, `pantry`, `greenhouse`, `journal`, `toolshelf`, `shortlinks` and `events`) gains a `README.md`: what the program does, what each file shows with links to the construct and configuration pages, what `una check` proves for it, its edition, and the commands to check, test, run and build it, with what it prints. `crates/una-cli/tests/guide.rs` requires a README in every example, runs its commands in a fresh copy of the package, compares what `una run` and the native executable print, checks that its Edition part names the edition of the package's `una.toml`, and checks that every relative link leads to a file. `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that renders each README as the prose of its example page, above the files, fences every file it shows by its longest run of backticks, and fails its build when a code fence on a page is closed early or never (website#16).

## v0.3.11

A patch of the Beta, with no security fix in `una`, that adds two designs to the edition `0.3` preview. A package can lock the `uses` of its public functions in `una.toml`, in a table `[contracts]` (ADR 0092 rule 8): a header may only drop or strengthen what its lock grants, and anything more is U3023, so a change that widens the authority of a public function also has to change the lock. The first part of module boundaries lands (ADR 0093): `[boundaries]` names modules and the ceiling of capabilities and operations that each one's extent may use, checked against the package (U7002), and `with @x = @y[ops]` rebinds a capability restricted to some of its operations. The checks of calls into a boundary follow in part 2. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged. The website that the release gate builds indexes long pages by section, so its search worst case falls from 147.7 to 130.9 KiB of its 150 KiB budget, and its construct pages show their editions as "0.1 and later" or "0.2 and later". Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.11-validation.md`.

- **Locked contracts in `una.toml`** (#564, ADR 0092 rule 8 and its third amendment). In edition `0.3`, a table `[contracts]` locks the `uses` of public functions: each key a function's qualified name, each value its `uses` clauses as a header writes them, operations listed, conditions included. The header may only drop or strengthen what the lock grants. A binding that the lock does not name, the locked binding renamed or a binding of another capability included, and an operation that the lock does not list, are U3023. A header condition that does not imply the lock's is U3023 through a new obligation kind, `contract_lock`. A lock that no longer resolves against the code, such as a renamed function, an unknown capability or operation, a condition over a name that is no parameter, or an entry without its operations, is U7002 at the entry. A capability is the declaration its name resolves to. Handlers are not locked: their operations are charged where they are installed. One pass over the locks and the locked headers, linear in their size. In editions `0.1` and `0.2`, `[contracts]` stays an unknown table.
- **Module boundaries, part 1: `[boundaries]` and narrowed rebindings** (#565, ADR 0093 with its amendment of 2026-10-13). In edition `0.3`, `una.toml` gains `[boundaries]`: each key is a module, which covers the modules below it, and each value its ceiling, names-only `C[ops]` strings. Overlapping keys, unknown modules, capabilities and operations, a boundary that is also trusted, and a `[run] entry` inside a boundary are U7002. `with @x = @y[ops]` rebinds `@x` to `@y` restricted to `ops` (U3002 for an operation `@y` does not permit, U1001 for one its capability lacks), keeping `@y`'s conditions. The checks of calls into a boundary (U3020, U3021, U3024) follow in part 2. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged.
- **The site gate indexes long pages by section, and edition badges read "and later"** (#633). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that indexes each page with more than 10,000 characters of searchable text per `##` section, so a search result links to the section and the largest search fragment stays small: the gate's search worst case falls from 147.7 to 130.9 KiB (English) of its 150 KiB budget (website#11). A construct or configuration page whose `editions` list runs up to the newest stable edition now reads "0.1 and later" or "0.2 and later", in its badge, its Markdown version and `llms.txt`; the website reads the preview editions from `spec/13 §7` and fails its build when it cannot (website#9).

## v0.3.10

A patch of the Beta, with no security fix in `una`, that lands the first rules of the edition `0.3` preview. Edition `0.3` now accepts `where` on an operation of a header's `uses`, `Catalog[journal where not dry_run]`, which only restricts: a new obligation, `effect_condition`, proves that the operation runs only where the condition holds (ADR 0092). Edition `0.3` also rejects failure handling on code that cannot fail: an `if error` clause there is U3025, which `una fix` removes, and `attempt` of such a call is U3026 (ADR 0100). Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged. The preview promise is stated precisely: for the programs that edition `0.2` can express, edition `0.3` only gets stricter, and a construct that a rule targeting edition `0.3` adds, such as a condition on an operation, only narrows what a program may do, since erasing it gives a program that edition `0.2` accepts (`spec/13 §7`, ADR 0056). The guide's construct and configuration pages are complete (#585), and the release gate builds the website from them, with every link checked; the website's build dependencies are past their advisories (S-19). Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.10-validation.md`.

- **Construct pages: capabilities** (#585, fourth batch). `docs/guide/language/capabilities/` gains `capability` and `op`, `uses`, `@` bindings and `with`, `handler`, and host capabilities with their grants, each with the template's seven parts and every block checked.
- **Construct pages: information flow, requirements and examples** (#585, fifth batch). `docs/guide/language/information-flow/` gains labels, `policy` and `trusted` modules; `docs/guide/language/requirements/` gains `requirement` and `derived from`; `docs/guide/language/examples/` gains `example`. Each page has the template's seven parts, and every block is checked. The `attempt` and `if error` pages state that, by ADR 0100, failure handling on code that cannot fail is an error in edition `0.3` (U3025 and U3026, below); editions `0.1` and `0.2` accept it.
- **Configuration pages** (#585, sixth batch). `docs/guide/configure/` gains one page per `una.toml` key: `[package]` `name`, `version` and `edition` (with the preview edition `0.3`), `[labels] categories`, `[trusted] modules`, `[escape_budget] checked_per_module`, `[run] entry` and `grants`, and one for `verification.lock`. Each page has the template's seven parts, and every block is checked. The guide test now keeps a `verification.lock` that a page shows, instead of replacing it with the repository's, so the samples of a lock naming another solver version (U5003) and of a lock missing a key (U7004) are checked as written.
- **Conditions on operations, in edition `0.3`** (#564, ADR 0092 rule 1). An operation of a function's or a handler's `uses` may carry `where` and a condition over the parameters: `uses @catalog: Catalog[price, journal where not dry_run]`. The condition has the restrictions of a `requires` clause: a `Bool` (U2001) of the refinement language (U2014, so no `checked`, `attempt`, operation or lambda), no opaque predicate (U2016), no `result` (U2012) and no `it` (U2013, "`it` names no value in an operation's condition"). A function type's `uses` keep the names-only form, so `where` there is U0101 in every edition, and a named function with a condition is not a function value (U3006); a lambda that calls it is. `una core` exports the conditions under an additive `conds` key of a `uses` entry, present only when one is written, and the formal track's re-check refuses a conditioned operation until it models them. Messages that named edition `0.2` as the edition of a rule now say "from edition `0.2`". Edition `0.3` now accepts `where` on an operation of a header's `uses`, which edition `0.2` and earlier releases reject as a syntax error; it only restricts, since erasing every condition gives a program that edition `0.2` accepts. `spec/13 §7` and ADR 0056 now state this: the preview promise that edition `0.3` only gets stricter concerns the programs edition `0.2` can express, and each construct that a rule targeting edition `0.3` adds must have this erasure property. The edition test pins the one edition `0.2` package that now differs, with the exact result of both editions and its erasure twin.
- **Conditions on operations are proven** (#564, ADR 0092 rules 3 and 5, with two amendments). A new obligation kind, `effect_condition`, proves that a conditioned operation runs only where its condition holds: at the operation, at a call that passes the binding (the callee's condition must imply the caller's; a callee without one, such as a private helper, needs the caller's condition at the call), at a `with` that installs a handler using it, and, by the amendments, at a call of a function value, whose type has no conditions, at a policy call (charged as a call of its `via`), at a call of a `uses of` parameter, and where a function is passed to a library function or to a `uses of` parameter. A callee the checker does not know is charged with every condition, so the check fails closed. A condition reads the arguments as the caller passed them, whatever the body later assigns. An operation in a lambda is charged where the lambda is defined, and a handler's operations against its own header. A refuted or unknown obligation is U5001 or U5002, "`@catalog.journal` may run where `not dry_run` is not proven", with repairs that add a condition to the callee, guard the site or add `requires`, and never remove a condition. One query per site, linear in the sites; code that writes no condition gets none.
- **Failure handling on code that cannot fail** (#602, ADR 0100). In edition `0.3`, an `if error` clause on an expression that cannot fail is U3025, with a repair that `una fix` applies (delete the clause), and `attempt` of a call that cannot fail is U3026, with a described repair. A dead clause's own U3008 and U3009 give way to U3025. The two codes wait while the package has an error that can hide a failure: any error outside U3007, U3008, U3009, U3025 and U3026, or, beside those, a site that is not plain (a call of a function value, a function-valued argument, or a poisoned callee). This covers a call of a function whose `fails of` parameter is given a function that cannot fail: such a call cannot fail either, so in edition `0.3` its `attempt`, for example `attempt relay(f: fn(n) -> n)`, is U3026, and an `if error` clause on it is U3025. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged.
- **The site gate builds the construct pages** (#627). `site/website.lock` pins una-lang/website at the commit that renders the construct and configuration pages from their sources in the docs bundle, checks each page's structure as `crates/una-cli/tests/guide.rs` does, and checks every link of the built site (website#8). Its build dependencies are past their high-severity advisories: sharp 0.35.5, vite 7.3.7 and esbuild 0.28.2 (website#12, S-19).

## v0.3.9

A patch of the Beta, with no security fix, that opens edition `0.3` as a preview. A package may now declare `edition = "0.3"`: for now it has every construct and rule of edition `0.2`, and the rules of ADRs 0092, 0093 and 0094 join it as they land, so a later `0.3.x` release may reject an edition `0.3` package that this one accepts. It only gets stricter, never looser. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged, and are the editions to use for code that must keep checking. The release also carries the first construct pages of the guide (#585): error handling, types and contracts, and control flow, each checked against the specification by a test. The inferred error kind of a private function follows the body's source order (ADR 0099), so the site and code of a conflict no longer depend on where declarations stand. ADR 0100 is accepted: in edition `0.3`, a handling clause or an `attempt` on code that cannot fail will be an error, once it is implemented. A change to what the Linux local-ci runs now needs that run before it merges (#605). Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.9-validation.md`.

- **Edition `0.3`, a preview** (#564, ADR 0056 amendment). `edition = "0.3"` is accepted in `una.toml`, and `una --version` lists it. It has every construct, keyword and rule of edition `0.2`, which `spec/13 §7` now states as a rule: editions are cumulative. The rules of ADRs 0092, 0093 and 0094 target it and join it as they land, so until then a later `0.3.x` release may add obligations or errors to an edition `0.3` package: it only gets stricter, never looser. A test rechecks every edition `0.2` package of the conformance suite as edition `0.3` and requires byte-identical output, with an explicit list for the differences the later rules bring. Editions `0.1` and `0.2` are unchanged; the one message that changes is U7002's list of editions, for an edition that is not supported.
- **Construct pages: error handling** (#585, first batch). `docs/guide/language/errors/` gains one page each for `fails`, `fail`, `if error` and `if none`, `attempt` and `checked` with the escape budget. Each page has the seven parts of the website's template: what it does, forms with their spec/02 production, examples, edge cases, diagnostics, edition and normative source. Every statement cites its spec section, and every failing example names its code. `crates/una-cli/tests/guide.rs` checks each page: the template's parts in order, front matter whose codes match the Diagnostics part and have explain texts, and every block, as the guides' are.
- **Construct pages: types and contracts** (#585, second batch). `docs/guide/language/types/` gains `kind`, `where` and refinements, `predicate` (`reflect` and `opaque`) and constraints on generics; `docs/guide/language/functions/` gains `requires` and `ensures`. Each page has the template's seven parts, and every block is checked.
- **Construct pages: control flow** (#585, third batch). `docs/guide/language/control-flow/` gains `when`, `for`, `let edit` and `edit` parameters, `take` and `together`, each with the template's seven parts and every block checked.
- **The inferred error kind follows source order** (#609, ADR 0099). A private function without `fails` takes the kind of the first failure of its body in source order, a raised variant or a passed failure (`spec/04 §5` rule 3). A pass of a callee whose kind is inferred now waits for that kind, so moving a declaration no longer changes which conflicting site is reported, or whether it is U3009 or U3008. Functions that pass each other's failures decide together. Every edition: what is accepted and rejected does not change, only the site and code of a conflict.

## v0.3.8

A feature patch of the Beta, with no security fix: it carries what was validated on `main` since v0.3.7. Repairs rank by what they cost: escape repairs last (ADR 0095), widening repairs after the ones that keep authority (ADR 0097), foreign syntax gets its exact fix and one check reports past a broken declaration (ADR 0096), and an unknown name reports one diagnostic, at the reference (#581). `una core --with-rejected` (ADR 0091) exports rejected programs, `una run` and `una test` say which tier they check (#556), and a call that keeps its argument live no longer counts references for a parameter it may consume (#284). Edition 0.3's first three designs are accepted (ADR 0092, 0093 and 0094), and the formal model now states authority confinement at module boundaries and proves trace safety, stage 1. The formal track's re-check reads `Result` error types, user record and sum kinds, `with` scopes, handlers, fixed-width integers and more of the prelude; the differential security hunt (#513) runs its generator families with no new finding; and the docs bundle feeds a site gate. Validation: `docs/releases/0.3.8-validation.md`.

- **A call that keeps its argument live no longer counts references for a parameter it may consume** (#284, ADR 0072 implementation note). Natively, a function that owns a parameter, because some path of it consumes it, now has a second entry that borrows every parameter, compiled only when a call uses it. A call takes that entry when none of its arguments of owned parameters is at its last use: before, it retained each one for the callee to release, even when the consuming path never ran. The copies are the same as before, and a call whose argument moves still takes the owned entry, so appending through calls stays linear. `cons(xs, i) = if i < 0 then xs.append(item: i).len else xs.len`, called 10^7 times with `xs` live: 1.600 s on main, 1.274 s now, against 1.218 s before ADR 0072; the remaining 4.6% is a per-call cost that a function with an `Int` parameter shows too (6.0% since then, unchanged by this change).
- **Edition 0.1's last reading of `T*` as `T` is gone** (#356). Since #354 both editions tell `ℓ*_T` from `ℓ_T` in every flow, so the flag that chose between them and the branch of the label order that erased the star were dead code; they are removed. No behavior change: the 212 `hof_` conformance cases of the function-value matrix, each run in both editions, give byte-identical diagnostics before and after (424 runs, no verdict changes). Edition 0.1's own rules for decided arguments stay, and two new conformance cases pin one of them: a function value whose parameter is `label(T)` rejects an argument decided by values of `T` in edition 0.1 (U4001), where edition 0.2 accepts it and raises the call instead.
- **The docs bundle, and the site gate builds the website from it** (#584). `scripts/site-bundle.sh OUT` writes the documents the website renders: the guides, the specification, the explain pages, the examples, the root documents, the ADRs and the release's validation record, each at its repository path. With them it writes a `manifest.json` (`una.site-bundle/1`: version, commit, editions, and per file its kind, title, digest, spec references and neutrality), the built binary's `--help` texts and a reproducible archive with its digest. Neutrality is checked against a pattern read only from `UNA_NEUTRALITY_PATTERN`. The release gate `site` now also writes the bundle and runs `scripts/site-website.sh`, which builds una-lang/website at the commit `site/website.lock` pins from that bundle, with its budget and code samples checked; the validation record lists the bundle's digest. mdBook stays until a release has built the new site, then retires in its own change.
- **T6 covers the environment's secrets** (#487, `docs/plan/formal-track-env.md`). The formal model's environment moves from the host into the world, so two runs may read different values from `Env.get`, and T6 relates them: a program that leaks a variable's value now has no typing, which a theorem shows, and one that keeps it `internal` is certified with runs from different environments. The premise is that the same variables exist in both runs, since whether a variable exists is `public` (`spec/14 §3`): it is public configuration, so an operator must not encode a secret in which variables are set. The note of a declaration that reads `Env` and the guide's `Env` section say so. Corpus: no verdict changes (756 accepted, none rejected).
- **The replay covers S-05** (#498, `docs/reviews/formal-m2-replay/README.md`). S-05's five leaking shapes, dumped by v0.1.3, the last release before its fix, are read with `Files` and with the file-state label that v0.3.6 infers for the same repro, the one input not taken from the release. The re-check rejects 4 of them and accepts the secure control; the fifth branches on `FileError`'s variants, outside F1, and is not re-checked. On these repros S-05's rule and ADR 0085's file-state term cannot be separated, and the README says why neither fixed label is faithful. S-03 is documented as not replayable from any release: `Http` first shipped in v0.3.0, after its fix.
- **The project and release repositories** (#483, ADR 0088). The design of the public release repository for v1.0: a checked-in mirror map with two-way, one-way projected and never-mirrored paths; release-facing text that is neutral and institutional, with attributions kept in the project repository; one fail-closed leak gate for every sync and release, whose lists stay outside every repository; a clean history from v1.0; contributions merged through the project repository; signed tags and minisign-signed archives; releases cut from the release repository.

- **The formal model releases a `via` function's failure** (#453, Phase B). A `via` function may now fail, as `checked` in it does: the policy call releases `Err(c)` and fails with `c`, and T-Declassify labels the failure `pc ⊔ ℓ(to)`, as ADR 0090 does for the language. T6 with releases, its erasure and host-free variants keep their statements; a release seen by the observer is equal in both runs, or they diverge there. The re-check reads such `via` functions, and on the corpus `via_result_checked_opaque`'s `app.publish` is accepted, one more than before, with none rejected. A `via` body that branches on a comparison of its parameter is not re-checked yet: the compiler's temporary keeps the parameter's label, which the check's retry does not lower.
- **The re-check reads a `Result`'s error type** (#507). The importer dropped it, but F1's `Err` is a code while `to_text` and `==` read an `Err`'s payload: on a Core that typed `to_text` of a `Result[Int, internal Int]` as `public`, the re-check said accepted. `una check` rejects such a source (U4001), so the corpus held no leak. Now a `Result` is read only when its error type is a kind without payload whose labels the result carries; otherwise its declaration is not re-checked. Corpus: 756 to 754 accepted, none rejected.
- **The formal model gives a `Result` its error type** (#508). F1's results were a value type and a label, with a failure's code in `Err`; `Result[T, E]` now keeps `E`, `Ok(a)` and `Err(a)` build a result, and a `case` binds the `Err` arm's value at `E` raised by the scrutinee's label. `to_text` and `==` take the labels of both positions, so #507's refusal becomes label tracking: S-04's `result_ok` and `result_err` are rejected at the leak (8 of 8 replayable shapes), and `ResultExample` proves non-vacuity, including a constructor chosen by an `internal` condition. A kind without payload is still a failure's code; failures with a payload are #512. `scripts/formal.sh`: 235 theorems.
- **The differential security hunt** (#513, `docs/plan/security-hunt.md`). A plan to find every information-flow leak: programs generated and mutated per `spec/07` rule, checked by `una check` and the formal re-check (a declaration one accepts and the other rejects is a candidate), the reverse comparison through an export of rejected declarations (#514), and a two-run noninterference oracle that runs each accepted program on inputs differing only in secret values and compares what a public or internal observer sees (`docs/plan/security-hunt-oracle.md`). The audit `docs/reviews/security-hunt/coverage.md` maps every rule of `spec/07` to the checker, the conformance cases and the model, and names the rules no theorem covers yet: handler bodies, `Keys.hmac`, kinds with type parameters, failure payloads, `together`, user sum types, trusted modules and policies outside B1.
- **The re-check reads user record kinds** (#509). The importer looked for a kind's type parameters under `type_params`, but the export writes `params`, so no user record was read. A record is now read when the export flags it a record, without type parameters, and when it does not reach itself (F1's equality of records is bounded, so `==` over a recursive record would be rejected where Una allows it). `RecordExample` proves non-vacuity. Corpus: in documents `una check` accepts, 506 to 529 accepted declarations. In all, 765 to 1,028, none rejected and none lost; 240 of the 263 new accepts are declarations of verification tests that `una check` rejects for a refuted refinement (U5001), whose information flow the re-check certifies. `scripts/formal.sh`: 243 theorems.
- **Conformance cases for `spec/07`'s untested corners** (#516, from the audit of #513). An `expect` line that reads a secret result, a `do` line at pc ⊥ and a branch inside one, `requires`, `ensures` and `where` clauses on secret values, the file-state label of a write inside a lambda (`internal`) and of one reached only from an example or written in an example's `do` line (`public`), and `sort` raising the result list itself. 33 label and variance sweep files that held only rejected declarations gain accepted twins at their ends, so an over-strict checker shows there too.
- **The security hunt's first generators** (#513, `scripts/security-hunt/`). Family T writes packages that break the rules of trusted modules and policies (`spec/07 §5`, §6), which the formal model assumes and only the checker enforces: twelve operators, from a listing in `una.toml` that spells the module otherwise to every way of reaching a `via` function other than through its policy, policies outside B1, policy calls under a raised program counter, and failing `via` functions (S-18). Families H (handler bodies and the effect floor) and K (`Keys.hmac`, ADR 0039) cover two more rules the model does not state. Two drivers judge with `una check` alone, in a sandbox: a program built to break a rule must be rejected, and a refactor that keeps every label must keep the verdict. Every program is rebuilt from its operator, seed and edition, and checked against its manifest's digest.
- **The security hunt's families P, G, C, S, O and F** (#513, `scripts/security-hunt/`). Generators for the blind rows of the coverage audit: failure payloads (P), kind parameters and invariance with recursive kinds (G), `together`, including cancellation seen by a sibling task (C), user sum types with `is` and `in` (S), and `Ord`, `sort`, `min` and `max` (O), each must-reject shape with a twin on public data; and F, programs inside the formal re-check's vocabulary, with a `differ` driver that compares `una check` with `una-core-check` (part b). Two campaigns against v0.3.7 (seeds 4 and 5, 1,056 programs) found no leak: every must-reject program was rejected with its expected code, every open program of P to O was accepted, R6 and R9 kept every verdict, and the re-check rejected no declaration of an F program that `una check` accepts.
- **The hunt's `differ` reports a rejection the re-check printed before it stopped** (#513). A re-check that printed a rejected declaration and then timed out or crashed was judged `unknown`, which hid the rejection; it is now a finding, whose detail says how the run ended.
- **The security hunt's composed families CS, PG, TC, CP and GS** (#513, `scripts/security-hunt/`). Two blind rows at once: `together` over user sum types (CS: tasks matching secret sum values or reading a secret variant field, a branch value kept after the block, a secret `is` deciding the task count) and failure payloads holding generic kinds (PG: a covariant `Box[secret Int]`, the same in `Option`, an invariant `Sink[Int]` taken as `Sink[secret Int]`), policies called inside tasks, including a failing `via` whose failure cancels a sibling task (TC), payload failures in tasks (CP), and generic sum kinds at secret instances (GS); each with a twin on public data. Seeds 8 and 9 against v0.3.7 (1,200 programs): every must-reject program rejected with its expected code (976), every open one accepted (224), and R6 and R9 kept every verdict.

- **`una core --with-rejected`** (#514, ADR 0091). With `--json`, the Core export can also carry the declarations that the checker rejected for information flow only, under a key of their own, `rejected`, with their diagnostics; they are never in `modules`, and without the flag the document is unchanged. The formal re-check reads them in a reverse mode (`una-core-check --rejected`), whose outcomes are `model-accepts-candidate`, `model-rejects` and `not-re-checked`, for the security hunt's reverse comparison (#513).
- **The formal model reads the arithmetic primitives** (#526). `mul`, `div` and `mod` join F1's primitives, with Euclidean division as `spec/11` defines it, and the importer reads `neg`, `le`, `ge` and `ne` through the primitives F1 has; each result carries its operands' labels, as `add`'s does. A zero divisor is a new outcome, `aborted`: a typed program still never gets stuck (T1), and an abort is outside T6's premise, which is termination-insensitive (`spec/07 §1`). A divisor that depends on a secret and escapes the divisor obligation (U5001) is a termination channel, as the fixed-width overflow abort is. `ArithExample` proves non-vacuity.
- **The replay of the findings is restored, and gated** (#528). #514's fail-closed reading refused the replay's old dumps, which predate the keys #443 added, so the replay showed no rejection. The converter now writes each key only where it is a fact of the dump's release, and the replay matches #509's but for two S-04 shapes whose generic helpers' summaries v0.2.0 did not print (6 of 8 rejected). Local CI compares the replay with its recorded output byte for byte.
- **The formal model reads sum kinds and failures with a payload** (#512). F1 gains a kind's variants with fields, `fail C(...)` with arguments, `attempt` and handlers that receive the failure's value, and `when` over a sum, read as a chain of tests that the importer checks against the declared variants itself. A field keeps its declared label, so a secret field of a public failure is never read into a public sink. Equality on a sum, a function value that fails with a payload, and a policy whose `via` function does are not re-checked; nothing is accepted by default.
- **The re-check types a `via` function whose temporaries keep its parameter's label** (#510). `una core` exports a temporary computed from a `via` function's parameter with that parameter's label, such as `let %t0: secret Bool = x > 3` before an `if`, so the structural typing of the via body, which lowered only the parameters' labels, gave up ("no structural typing"). It now also lowers the top labels of the body's `let` annotations: a via function's labels are not checked (`spec/07 §5` rule 3), and structural typing reads no annotation, so this is completeness only. Corpus: 1,131 to 1,135 accepted, none rejected, none lost; the 4 are the `s18_ok_via_failure_public_to` controls in both editions, which leaves no via function without a structural typing. `scripts/formal.sh`: 259 theorems.
- **The formal model reads `prelude.first`** (#532, `docs/plan/formal-track-first.md`). `first(xs)` is its own form in F1: its option carries the top label of `xs`, and the head keeps its own label inside it (`spec/14 §1`), so a public list of secret elements matched only for `Some` or `None` is certified, and printing its head is rejected. T1, T6, erasure and the host-free simulation gain the case; `FirstExample` proves non-vacuity, and `FirstImportTests` reads `una`'s own export. A primitive on operands F1 does not model, such as `<` on `Text`, which Una orders, an `==` whose sides F1 reads at different types, and `to_text` or `==` of a type without equality in F1, is now an F1 restriction, not re-checked, where the re-check rejected it. Corpus: 1,162 to 1,173 accepted, none rejected; the 11 are declarations that call `first`, all in documents `una check` accepts. The hunt's family F on seeds 5 to 7 reaches the 92 declarations that called `first`, with no finding. `scripts/formal.sh`: 291 theorems.
- **The re-check reports refinement predicates in their own category** (#532). A `pred` declaration, such as the prelude's, which every export carries, was "not re-checked: a `pred` declaration"; it is outside the flow model by design, since a refinement predicate is pure and its labels are erased (`spec/07 §7` rule 6). `una-core-check` now prints it as `outside the flow model`, counted apart from `not re-checked` and never dropped; it is never accepted, and a function that reaches one is not re-checked. The hunt's `differ` reads the new outcome.
- **A breadth run of the security hunt, seed 11** (#513). Every family and operator in both editions (5,922 programs), and family F (126 programs plus a batch of 1,000), against v0.3.7: every must-reject program rejected with its expected code (4,524), every open one accepted (1,398), R6 and R9 kept every verdict they apply to, and the re-check found no difference on the 462 F programs `una check` accepts. The hunt's `differ` now also expects a line for each refinement predicate, so one dropped from the re-check's output is `unknown` (#532).
- **The hunt's coverage audit, updated** (#513, `docs/reviews/security-hunt/coverage.md`). After #508, #509, #510, #512, #526, #532 and #514, failure payloads, `case` on user sums and failures at a public pc move from blind or partial to covered or partial, the explicit flows read 16 primitives, and `via` bodies that branch on a comparison are covered; a new section lists each change and the families that ran the rows still blind. The `differ` now also requires a refinement predicate's line to say `outside the flow model`, unless the whole document fell back to one reason.
- **The formal model reads the handlers of user capabilities** (#524, `docs/plan/formal-track-handlers.md`). An operation whose one handler the document declares is invoked as a call of a synthesized function, `module.handler.op`, whose parameters carry the operation's `accepts`; the whole-program check holds every handler to the effect floor, and a caller's certificate reads only the operation's signature, so it holds for every handler `una check` accepts. An invocation's failure is labeled at the caller's program counter, and, by ruling D, `attempt` and a handler label what they give by the failure's decision label, what decides it without the program counter: an `if` or `case` joins its condition into it. T6's statement is unchanged; its proof relates failures at the decision label's visibility. `HandlerExample` and `DecisionExample` prove non-vacuity, `HandlerImportTests` reads `una`'s own export. A declaration that installs a handler with `with`, or invokes an operation whose handler the document does not declare, is not re-checked (#538). Corpus: 1,173 to 1,176 accepted source declarations, none rejected; the 3 are invokers of handlers in documents whose only `una check` errors, if any, are verification ones; the 19 synthesized handler lines are reported apart (17 accepted, 2 not re-checked). `scripts/formal.sh`: 323 theorems.
- **The formal model reads `abs`, `min`, `max`, `slice`, `or_else` and `is_ok`** (#542, `docs/plan/formal-track-prelude-six.md`). `abs`, `min`, `max` and `slice` are primitives whose result carries every label of their arguments, as `spec/14 §1` labels them; `slice` outside its precondition aborts, the termination residual of a zero divisor, which T6 does not claim. `or_else` raises its result by every label of both arguments, which over-approximates a structured `T` as Una itself does, so the reverse comparison gains no candidate from it; `is_ok` is structural, its result carrying the top label of the result only. Edition 0.2's `min` and `max` on another type than `Int` are an F1 restriction, not re-checked. T6's statement is unchanged; `PreludeExample` proves non-vacuity, `PreludeImportTests` reads `una`'s own export, and the replay rejects 4 more S-04 shapes at the leak (38 to 42). Corpus: 1,176 to 1,196 accepted source declarations, none rejected; 15 of the 20 are in documents `una check` accepts, 5 in documents whose only errors are refuted refinements (U5001); the synthesized handler lines are unchanged. `scripts/formal.sh`: 341 theorems.
- **The formal model reads `prelude.at`** (#545, `docs/plan/formal-track-at.md`). `at(xs, index)` is its own form: its option carries the top label of `xs` and the label of `index`, which decide whether there is an element, and the element keeps its own label inside it (`spec/14 §1`); evaluation is total. T6's statement is unchanged; `AtExample` proves non-vacuity, a secret index deciding a public print included, and `AtImportTests` reads `una`'s own export. The replay rejects the last 3 S-04 shapes that stopped at `at` (42 to 45; S-04: 10 to 13). Corpus: 1,196 to 1,199 accepted source declarations, none rejected, all 3 in documents `una check` accepts. `scripts/formal.sh`: 352 theorems.
- **The security hunt's family F reaches the new prelude functions** (#513). F7 routes a secret through `at`, at a public and at a secret index, `or_else`, with a secret value and with a secret default, `min`, `max`, `abs`, `slice`, and `is_ok`, of a public result of a secret, which is structural, and of a result chosen under a secret condition; a table of its own, so F1's programs for earlier seeds rebuild unchanged. Seed 12 against the main of #546 (5,922 programs of the other families, and 2,126 of F: 126, a batch of 1,000, and 1,000 of F7): every must-reject program rejected with its expected code (4,545), every open one accepted (1,377), R6 and R9 kept every verdict they apply to, and the re-check found no difference on the 1,087 F and F7 programs `una check` accepts; F7's verdicts follow the labels exactly, public endings rejected and secret ones accepted, `is_ok` of a public result accepted at every ending. A public ending of F1 and F7 is now must-reject, so `una check` alone decides it whatever the re-check says, and F7's structural `is_ok` there is must-accept; `differ` judges a program's own declaration not re-checked `unknown` (found in review); the programs' text is unchanged, so earlier seeds rebuild to their digests. An audit of every F run so far (4,434 programs, seeds 5 to 12) found no public ending `una check` accepted but those of `is_ok`, which are correct.
- **The formal model reads `with` scopes** (#538, `docs/plan/formal-track-with.md`). A capability a document's `with` installs is read by scopes: each operation has a marker, a public text naming the handler installed for it, which a `with` binds and every call passes on, so a function's invocations are bound by its callers' scopes; an invocation runs the handler its marker names, or the operation's representative, so a typed invocation never gets stuck. By ruling E, a handler's events are charged where it is installed: an effect *emits* when it has its own event or invokes an operation whose handlers print, and a `with` discharges the operation and charges the installed handler's own event. Handlers of one operation must agree, by flows, on their signature, `accepts` and whether they print, or the program is not certified. T1 and T6 keep their statements. `WithExample` proves non-vacuity (a printing handler installed under a secret program counter is rejected and leaks; at a public one it is certified; an invocation no scope installs has no typing; a quiet handler installed under a secret program counter is certified and prints the same in both runs; a failing handler chosen under a secret program counter is rejected and leaks), `WithImportTests` reads `una`'s own export (the hunt's H3 shapes, two handlers by scope, C5(b) rejected, a document naming a host capability refused). The re-check combines this reading with #524's, which no longer accepts an operation of a capability that `with` scopes install, read without them. Corpus: 1,199 to 1,194 accepted source declarations, none rejected: 1 gained, and 6 that reach `Log.write` outside its scopes no longer accepted, all in documents `una check` accepts; the synthesized handler lines are unchanged (17 accepted, 2 not re-checked). `scripts/formal.sh`: 366 theorems.
- **The formal model reads fixed-width integers** (#548, `docs/plan/formal-track-fixed.md`, phase 1). A fixed-width type is F1's `Int` at its label; each arithmetic step at a fixed-width type, and each conversion to one, is the exact `Int` result followed by a new primitive, `fit`, which aborts outside the type's range, as Una's runtime does where an `overflow` obligation is not proven. Two aborts stay outside T6, which is termination-insensitive: an overflow (ADR 0057) and a zero divisor (#526); neither is claimed. In a function with fixed-width values, arithmetic on operands of mixed or unknown integer types is not re-checked, never read as unbounded `Int`; wrapping, saturating and bit methods stay outside F1. `FixedExample` proves non-vacuity, `FixedImportTests` reads `una`'s own export. Corpus, after #538: 1,194 to 1,218 accepted source declarations, none rejected; 14 of the 24 are in documents `una check` accepts, 10 in documents whose only errors are refuted obligations (U5001). `scripts/formal.sh`: 378 theorems.
- **The formal model's reading of `with` nodes is exact** (#551). A `with` node with exactly `kind`, `cap`, `source` and `body` is a scope, one with its kind alone is a map's `with` operation, and any other leaves the document not re-checked ("a `with` node this importer cannot read"), as does a scope's source that names no handler of the document. `WithImportTests` reads nodes without `source`, `body` or `cap`, and with a field besides the four, and a document with a map's `with`, which is still read.
- **The re-check classifies a refusal by its constructor** (#535). The checker's refusal is a rejection or a model restriction (`Check.Err`), and `classify` reads which, no longer whether the message contains `B1 restriction` or `F1 restriction`; an unmarked message is a rejection. `scripts/formal-restrictions.sh`, run by `scripts/formal.sh`, checks both ways that every restriction message is raised as one and every restriction says which, that the importer's marked refusals are tagged and outside the checker's result, and that no marker is split; `scripts/formal-restrictions-test.sh` runs 9 mutants against it. Messages, verdicts and theorem statements are unchanged.
- **Three accepted designs for edition 0.3: when an allowed operation runs, with which authority, and on whose behalf** (#520; ADR 0092, 0093 and 0094). Conditions on the operations of a binding (`Catalog[journal where not dry_run]`), checked at each site with implication at calls, and locked for public entry points in `una.toml` `[contracts]`; boundaries declared by the consumer in `una.toml` `[boundaries]` with a ceiling per module, no authority crossing them by name, and narrowed rebindings `@y[ops]`; labels on the operations of a binding (`owner gives internal[Service]`), which join into the answer and the decision on its failure. Codes U3020 to U3023 are assigned. Nothing is implemented yet (#564 to #566), the theorems are open (#561 to #563), and B04, B05 and B07 stay open questions (#558, #560); editions 0.1 and 0.2 do not change.
- **The security hunt's family F reaches fixed-width integers** (#553, N-138). F8 routes a secret through fixed-width arithmetic and conversions, which the re-check reads since #548, in tables of its own, so earlier seeds rebuild to their digests. A public ending of a secret is must-reject, a secret ending and public arithmetic must-accept. `differ` judges what the re-check says of the program's own declaration: arithmetic on a pattern's binder must be not re-checked for its operands' unknown types, and the wrapping, saturating and bit methods are out of F1, an expected `unknown` counted apart; either accepted is a finding. A new class, residual, holds programs whose abort a secret decides, a termination channel T6 does not claim: they are never judged clean, and the driver records `una check` at tier 0 and the full check, whose `overflow` and `division` obligations guard the channel, with the solver required; a public print either accepts goes to triage. In edition 0.1, F8's programs are must-reject (U1001). Seed 13, against the main of #552 (1,000 programs of edition 0.2, 50 of 0.1): every must-reject program rejected (474), every must-accept one accepted (351), 240 of them re-checked and accepted; the binder shapes not re-checked for their operands' types (40) and the methods out of F1 (71), none accepted; each residual shape (overflow 41, zero divisor 40, negation 50, narrowing 44) accepted at tier 0 and rejected by the full check with U5001 alone, so no full check accepted a public print after a secret-dependent abort; the 94 tier 0 acceptances of one are not findings, since `una run`, `una test` and `una build` check at tier 1; edition 0.1's 50 rejected with U1001. A residual row is invalid, and rerun, unless the full check ran with the solver to an acceptance or to a rejection by an obligation (U5001, U5002), and its rows say `residual`, never `clean` (Una-2's review); the stored rows, tallied again with that rule, give the same numbers, since no full check timed out, failed or was rejected otherwise. The first run of seed 13 annotated each `let` over the secret with a public type, which Una rejects at the `let` (U4001), so its fixed-width shapes tested nothing; the generator now annotates them `secret`, and a test keeps it so. The run's first solver probe used a hand-written sandbox profile that denied writes to `/dev/null`, so the solver could not start (U5003) and the batch did not run; the probe now goes through the hunt's own sandbox, and gave U5001 at tier 1.
- **`una run` and `una test` say which tier they check** (#556). Their doc comments said tier 0; both check at tier 1 before they evaluate, as they have since M8. Documentation only.
- **ADR 0093, amended: no release across a boundary** (#565). A call into a boundary module from which a release (a `declassify` or a policy call) is reachable is a build error, U3024, and a module cannot be both trusted and a boundary (U7002): a vendored or plug-in module never declassifies, so releases stay in the trusted modules. Found while stating authority confinement (#562); edition 0.3, implemented with #565.
- **The formal model states authority confinement at module boundaries** (#562, `docs/plan/formal-track-boundary.md`, ADR 0093 rule 7). `eval_confined` proves that every event of a run of code within a ceiling (`Auth`) is allowed by it, and that its values stay within it; a release is never allowed, as ADR 0093's amendment (U3024) requires. The four forms of the ADR's rule 3 follow: a call into the boundary module, its function used as a value (`funRef_within`, `apply_confined`), a handler declared there and installed outside, and a functional argument. `confinedB` checks a candidate set of functions, linear in its functions and call edges, and is sound (`confinedB_sound`). `BoundaryExample` gives six cases in the shapes of B10 to B12, each rejected one with a concrete run whose event is outside the ceiling. The importer reads an optional `boundaries` key, which the export does not write yet (R9), tested on synthesized documents; a ceiling it cannot read is not re-checked, and so is a call that grants a host capability's handler, reaches a release or passes an effectful closure across the boundary (R1 to R11). `Env.get` is checked statically only: it has no event, so `eval_confined`'s guarantee does not cover environment reads (R3). 399 theorems, no `sorry`; T6 unchanged; the corpus delta is 0 (1,235 accepted, 3,002 not re-checked, 113 outside the flow model, on both sides) and the replay is unchanged.
- **The formal model proves trace safety, stage 1** (#561, ADR 0092, `docs/plan/formal-track-trace-safety.md`). A function's header may condition an operation on its parameters (`Fun.conds`); a check (`Program.traceSafeB`) asks every site where the operation may be performed to imply its condition, at the operation, and at a call, an invocation or a handler where the callee's condition at the arguments is also known, decided by truth table over atoms identified by syntax. `trace_safety`: a run of a checked function on arguments where a condition is false never performs its operation, whatever the run's outcome. A user operation's performance is a ghost event (`Event.perf`, `Event.perfFn`) that no observer sees, so T6 and every other theorem keep their statements. In stage 1, a closure that may perform a conditioned operation is not re-checked (stage 2 is #568). `TraceSafetyExample` proves non-vacuity in B06's shape. Corpus and replay unchanged; `scripts/formal.sh`: 394 theorems.
- **Escape repairs rank last and say what they cost** (#574, ADR 0095). A repair that takes an escape hatch, `checked`, a raised `checked_per_module` or a trusted module or policy, comes after every repair that keeps the guarantee, and `--json` marks it with an optional `escape` key, `{"kind", "cost"}`, under `una.diagnostic/1`; ranks are numbered in output order, and the human output prints `repair N (escape: <kind>; <cost>)`, or `repair N:` for the others. U7003 no longer offers the raised budget as its first repair, U2016 offers a test without the fact where it applies, and U4003, U4004 and U7005 rank widening the trusted base last; the messages of U4003 and U4004 drop their imperative clause. In the Suite C full run, every Una run with an escape used `checked` and a raised budget, which U7003 offered at rank 1 without marking. A test checks the order and the marking on every conformance case.
- **Foreign syntax gets its exact fix, and one check reports past a broken declaration** (#576, #575, ADR 0096). The measurement of una-bench's Suite B and C runs found syntax alone in 72% of the Una agents' check outputs, from a dozen habits of other languages. Each of them stays an error (charter P4). Its first repair now carries an exact edit when the rewrite keeps its reading token for token and the declaration then parses: an `if … then … else` wrapped across lines, `=` at the end of a function header or indented under its clauses, a guard without `else` whose branch fails, a `when` with its arms on one line, an arm whose body starts on the arrow's line, `fn` and `priv` before a declaration, `;`, and a name that starts with `_`. A rename takes no name that is visible where it is written. `una fix` applies these edits under ADR 0084's rules. Lambdas over several lines, list patterns and tuples get Una's form in words. `una.diagnostics/1` gains `fixable`, the number of diagnostics `una fix` would repair, and human output says it on its last line. A file with a syntax error is now resolved and typed without its broken declarations. Their names are unknown rather than U1001, the declarations that reach them report only their syntax, and each other declaration is checked for canonical form on its own. A lambda given to an unknown or broken callee now takes the error type, so its parameters no longer report U2010 for what the missing callee caused. Checking past the gate also shows errors it used to hide: a conformance case whose `kind` aliases an unknown type now reports that U1001 beside its syntax error. A file with any error still never builds or runs.
- **Widening repairs rank after the ones that keep authority, and say what they widen** (#579, ADR 0097).
